`.planning/research/SUMMARY.md`

## How to read the checkboxes

**69 of 72 are `[x]`.** That is one MORE than the 68 that were checked before the v1.0
milestone audit — the first time this figure has been above its own baseline, and it is
above it for a reason worth saying out loud rather than reading as pure progress:
`AOT-05` is checked now and was not then, so the count has passed 68 while one of the
original 68 is still open in its place. Of the 3 now unchecked, **0 moved** and **3 were never checked**;
none of the 3 moved because the work was undone, and in fact none of the 3 moved at all. **The moved/never split read `2` and `4`
against 6 unchecked until 2026-08-19 and both figures were wrong, and the guard that checks
them was the reason.** `requirements-ledger.node.test.ts` derived `moved` as
`68 − <checked>` — an identity that holds only while every box now `[x]` was also `[x]`
before the audit, and `AOT-05` broke it: it is one of the four rows that were never checked
before the audit and it has since been ticked, so the subtraction counted a row that never
moved. The four are `AOT-03`, `AOT-05`, `BENCH-06` and `NET-03`, read out of the last
revision of this file whose v1 section holds 68 `[x]`; three of them are still open. And
**every** row that ever moved is now closed: `AUTH-03`, `MR-02` and `VER-09` all shut
on 2026-08-19, so the three still open are exactly the three that were never checked before
the audit. `moved` is therefore 0, which is the first time it has been, and it is the reading
that says nothing in this ledger is unticked because work was undone. The guard now derives the split from that membership rather than from
a subtraction, so it can disagree with the tree instead of only with itself. The one row
that ever did — VER-02,
whose box was cleared on 2026-07-30 because the mechanism behind it was found to check
nothing and was deleted — is checked again as of 2026-08-11, over a different mechanism.
Read this before drawing a conclusion from the count.

**VER-02 crossed *Not started* → *Partial* → `[x]` inside three days, and the middle
state is the one worth keeping in view.** It became *Partial* on 2026-08-11 with the
ceremony built, its refusals reachable and each watched red, and its serving leg measured
across spawned `bin/agent.ts` processes — and stayed `[ ]` because no runnable *submitter*
handed `submitJob` an all-committing executor set. `bin/bench.ts`'s timing wrapper now
forwards the two rounds, the ladder was re-measured on both sides of that change, and the
driver prints per rung how many rounds it dispatched — so the box moved on a reading from
a binary rather than on the ceremony existing. A bucket of zero is worth watching for the
opposite reason a bucket of one is: the split read `1 + 20 + 0` on 2026-08-11 and reads
`0 + 3 + 0` today, and a zero that stops being checked is a zero that can silently become
a one. **That sentence said "now reads `1 + 20 + 0`" until 2026-08-14**, three moves after
it stopped being true — the stale-claim shape this file exists to catch, committed in the
paragraph warning about it. **And it then said `0 + 9 + 0` until 2026-08-18, by which time
the tree read `0 + 7 + 0`** — so the same paragraph carried the same defect twice. The
reason it can is worth naming rather than apologising for: this is the ONLY figure in the
header that no regex in `requirements-ledger.node.test.ts` parses, because it is a
historical reading rather than a current one, and an unparsed number in this document is a
number that goes stale in silence. It is re-derived here against the tree at the same
moment as the two parsed ones beside it, which is the only discipline available to it.

**The count went UP by four on 2026-08-05**, which had not happened before in this
milestone, and it is worth saying which four and why: `CHURN-01`, `CHURN-02`, `CHURN-05`
and `CHURN-06` were delivered on the one job path by Phase 20 and each is measured across
real spawned `bin/agent.ts` processes rather than in a kernel fixture. `CHURN-03` and
`CHURN-04` moved out of *Built, not wired* in the same pass without their boxes moving,
because each has one leg no runnable entry point reaches — no production submitter
supplies `SubmitOptions.checkpoints`, and only `bin/bench.ts --discover`, off by default,
supplies the `admit` a lease renewal needs. `WIRE-04` is ticked too and is not in this
count, being a v1.1-only id.

The v1.0 milestone audit (`v1.0-MILESTONE-AUDIT.md`) traced every requirement from the
five runnable entry points — `bin/agent.ts`, `bin/seed.ts`, `bin/bench.ts`,
`tools/aot/cli.ts`, and the demo page — to the mechanism meant to satisfy it. Sovereignty
labelling, tree-reduce, discovery, quorum composition, capability chains and the entire
churn coordinator are **implemented, exported, and covered by their own specs** — and no
path a person can run reaches any of them.

So the ledger now distinguishes three states:

| Marker | Means |
|---|---|
| `[x]` | Delivered on a path reachable from a runnable entry point |
| `[ ]` + **Built, not wired** | Mechanism exists and is unit-verified; nothing calls it |
| `[ ]` + **Partial** | One leg reaches production, another does not |
| `[ ]` + **Refuted** | Measured and came back negative, unrepairably — not behind schedule, false. Added 2026-08-28 with `HOST-10`, whose claim is an *ordering* (the billing alert precedes the first Durable Object) that no later work can make true |

**The 3 unchecked boxes are 0 + 3 + 0**, and the three markers are not one label:
0 are *Built, not wired*, 3 are *Partial*, and 0 are *Not started*.
(The grammar of that first figure is awkward and is left alone deliberately: the sentence
is parsed back out of itself by `requirements-ledger.node.test.ts`, so its shape is the
assertion rather than a copy of one.) **The first bucket is EMPTY as of 2026-08-14**, where
until 2026-08-05 there were seven — the six `CHURN-0*` rows left it that day, four of them
by being delivered and two by being re-categorised as *Partial* with the leg that is
actually open named. `MR-02` was the last, and it left by being **wired**: `bin/bench.ts`'s
`--sovereign` leg now calls `reduceSovereignJob`. The marker's whole purpose is to name
mechanisms nothing calls, and the milestone existed to empty it.
**`MR-02` came back on 2026-08-18 — as *Partial*, not to this bucket, and the distinction
is the whole reason there are three markers.** The owner's flag ruling of 2026-08-15
(`.planning/consults/2026-08-15-owner-ruling-off-by-default-flag.md`) does not say nothing
calls `reduceSovereignJob`; something does, at `bin/bench.ts`'s own `reduceSovereignJob(` call
inside its `--sovereign` region. It says that call is two
flags deep and a flag nobody sets is not a shipped entry point. *Built, not wired* would be a
false statement about the tree; *Partial* is the true one. The sentence above is kept because
it was true when written and because the bucket it describes is still empty.
**And `MR-02` closed again on 2026-08-19, over a different call site** — `bin/agent.ts`'s
sovereign coordinator leg, which is `reduceSovereignJob`'s second production caller and the
first with no flag in front of it. So the row has now been wired twice and unticked once, and
the untick was not a regression in the code: nothing was removed from `bin/bench.ts`, and its
`--sovereign` leg still calls the same function two flags deep. What changed both times was
what counts as an entry point.

**An empty bucket is not a finished milestone, and reading it as one would be the exact
error this table is built to prevent.** **Two of this paragraph's figures were stale on
2026-08-18 and are corrected in place rather than deleted, because each was true when it
was written and neither is parsed by anything — which is why they drifted.** It read
*"Nineteen rows are still unchecked"* and *"the reachability guard still reports 27 open
callable exports with no production caller"*. **Fourteen** rows are still unchecked, and this
sentence now names the same figure the parsed one above does rather than keeping a second
copy nothing reads, which is exactly how it came to say nineteen (it said **eleven** until
2026-08-18, when three rows were unticked under the flag ruling).
*(CORRECTED 2026-08-22 — **three** rows are unchecked in this section, which is the figure
the parsed sentence above already names, and **four** across the whole document: `NET-03`,
`BENCH-06`, `AOT-03` and `AOTW-06`, against 98 ticked. Read them rather than quote them:
`grep -c '^- \[ \] \*\*' .planning/REQUIREMENTS.md` and
`grep -c '^- \[x\] \*\*' .planning/REQUIREMENTS.md`. Fourteen was true until 2026-08-19,
by which date `AUTH-03`, `MR-02` and `VER-09` had all closed. That makes four readings of one
figure — nineteen, eleven, fourteen, three — and the cause has not moved and is stated in this
very sentence: nothing parses it. `requirements-ledger.node.test.ts` reads back
`MOVED_AND_NEVER` and `UNCHECKED_SPLIT` and nothing else here, so this copy will drift a fifth
time unless it is parsed too or the reader is sent to the parsed sentence instead.)*
The guard's undisposed residue is **0**: it moved 15 → 2 on 2026-08-18, when thirteen rows left `OPEN_FINDINGS` by
three routes the register's own head names and distinguishes — two wired, five retired from
a barrel, six re-caused into `DISPOSITIONS`; then 2 → 1 when `core/localDispatch` was wired;
then **1 → 0 later the same day, when `core/isComplete` was retired from `@o2/core`'s barrel
after the owner asked for its purpose to be reviewed and the review found the capability
redundant**. `OPEN_FINDINGS` is empty and Phase 22's criterion 1 closes — the working, and
the two plants taken against a closure-by-retirement, are in `22-VERIFICATION.md`'s third
amendment of 2026-08-18.
**This figure read 2 for some hours after it had become 0, which is
the drift this paragraph exists to record about itself**: nothing parses it, which is exactly
why it drifts, so it is corrected in place rather than deleted.
**AMENDED 2026-08-21 — the register is no longer empty and Phase 22's criterion 1 no longer
closes on this reading.** `core/signNameDelegation` was registered in `OPEN_FINDINGS` that
day as the mint half of the offline-root chain. The walked graph reaches no caller for it
because the one caller it has, `packages/demo/scripts/sign-kernel.ts`, is a build script
outside the corpus the guard walks and deliberately not an entry point — a fact about where
the instrument stops, not a claim that nothing calls the symbol. The undisposed residue is
therefore **1**, and two neighbouring figures moved with it: `DISPOSITION_CEILING` reads
**68**, against the 66 the `WIRE-02` row quotes, and the total-unreachable bound in
`reachability-guard.node.test.ts` was raised 66 → 67 → 68 → 69 across 2026-08-20 and
2026-08-21, each raise named to one symbol in the comment above it. Re-derive the triple with
`unreachableExports(barrelExports(), buildCallGraph(), ROOT)` rather than subtracting from
the 2026-08-18 figures, by the method the `WIRE-02` row already states: ceilings to 0, the
guard's own verdict read, restored by the surgical inverse and `cmp` at exit 0.
Every unchecked row still has *some* leg in production and another that does not, which is what *Partial* means, and an empty first
bucket still does not mean nothing is dormant. Those two counts measure
different things: this marker is about a **requirement** whose mechanism nothing calls,
and that one is about a **symbol** nothing calls. The last row leaving here says the first
kind is gone; it says nothing about the second.
**AOT-02 crossed from the first bucket to the second on 2026-08-04**, and unlike the
four below it, that one *was* because new work landed: Plan 21-01 gave `translationCid`
a production caller in `tools/aot/lift.ts`, so "nothing calls it" stopped being true of
it the moment the commit went in — which is how the ledger guard found it.
**Four rows crossed the same way on 2026-08-03** — VER-03,
VER-04, VER-09 and VER-10 — and not one of them because new work landed in that
commit. *Built, not wired* means **nothing calls it**, and by the end of Phase 19
`composeQuorum`, `attestationReceipt` and `classifyAttestation` each had a production
caller and two display surfaces. The marker had become a false statement about the
tree while each row's own prose described the callers underneath it, which is the
same shape as the five reasons corrected on 2026-08-01: the sentence moved and the
label did not.
Quoting a single figure for "unreached" merges two states that mean different things —
a mechanism nothing calls, and a mechanism half of which ships — and the merge is what
let the count drift. `requirements-ledger.node.test.ts` now asserts every number in this
paragraph against the boxes and the table, so a row that changes marker without the
prose changing with it fails rather than rots.

The traceability table at the bottom carries the specific reason for each, naming the
symbol that proves it — `runResilient` has no caller, `composeQuorum` has none, no
production site calls `discoverExecutors`, and so on. **That naming is now checked, not
just written**: the same guard reads each *Built, not wired* row's own "X has no caller"
sentence and fails if X has acquired a production call site. Five rows — AUTH-01,
AUTH-02, AUTH-04, SCHED-03, NET-06 — were corrected on 2026-08-01 after exactly that
drift went unnoticed for a day, every one of them understating what had shipped.

**Two examples that used to stand in this paragraph were themselves expired, which is
the point.** It read *"`EgressGuard` decorates no production transport, `RemoteExecutor`
sends no capability field"*. Measured 2026-08-01: `new EgressGuard(transport, …)` is in
both node constructors (`fabric-node.ts` and `browser-node.ts`, search `new EgressGuard(`) and in both
benchmark drivers, so the first was flatly false. The second was true only by accident of
its call sites — `RemoteExecutor` *does* put `capability: chain` on the wire
(`remote-executor.ts`, search `capability: chain`); what is true is that all six production construction sites
pass the `'dispatches-unauthenticated'` sentinel, so no chain leaves a production
dispatch today. *(SUPERSEDED 2026-08-22 — both halves of that second clause have since
expired, and the sentence is kept because expiring is exactly what it was written to warn
about. There are **eleven** non-test `new RemoteExecutor(` sites, not six —
`grep -rn "new RemoteExecutor(" packages tools --include="*.ts" | grep -v node_modules`,
discounting its own docblock in `remote-executor.ts` — and chains **do** leave production
dispatches. `discover-candidates.ts` builds each executor with
`options.dispatch === 'dispatches-unauthenticated' ? options.dispatch : options.dispatch(peerId)`,
and three production callers hand it a real `CapabilitySupplier`: `bin/agent.ts`'s sovereign
coordinator leg, `dispatch: sovereignSupplierFor`; `bin/bench.ts` under `--sovereign`; and
the demo page's `chainFor`, minted by `sovereignChainsFor`. The `AUTH-03` row already records
this, and `remote-executor.ts`'s own docblock retracted the claim on 2026-08-06 — so the
correction was available here for sixteen days before anybody read the two documents side by
side, which is the second lesson.)* A sentence about a mechanism and a sentence about its callers are
different sentences, and only the second one was checkable. The examples above were
replaced with ones the guard actually holds.

**There are no `file:line` citations in this document any more, and that is the third
answer to a defect the first two answers only postponed.** Commit `a5a70c7` corrected four
rows on 2026-08-02; three commits later, inside that same plan, `548e119` inserted fifteen
comment lines into `fabric-node.ts` and sixteen into `browser-node.ts`, and every
coordinate below those points was wrong before the plan had finished. Twenty-three were
then re-measured line by line on 2026-08-03 — no claim and no marker moved, only digits.
**Measured again on 2026-08-05: 41 of the 46 surviving coordinates landed on a blank line,
a closing brace, a `*/`, or prose inside a comment**, and the five that were still right
were right by accident of which files nobody had touched. The offsets were 51, 138 and 226
lines in three different directions, so no single correction could have been applied across
the file. Re-measuring a fourth time would have reset a clock rather than stopped it, so
every citation is now a **grep-able symbol or quoted string** — the answer
`vocabulary.node.test.ts` reached for its own exemptions, for the same reason, and the
answer this paragraph recommended and did not take. **Every underlying claim was re-derived
by symbol during the conversion and every one held**; the rot was documentary throughout,
which is exactly what made it survive three passes. `requirements-ledger.node.test.ts`
still reads a row's sentences and not its coordinates, and now there are no coordinates for
it to be silent about.

**Why the checkbox rather than only the table.** A requirement is a claim about what
the system does. "The placer cannot relocate a sovereign task" is true of a placer no
job runs through, and a `[x]` next to it would be the project's own recorded
anti-pattern — *a documented bound that is not enforced* — written into the ledger
itself. The work is real and the table says so; the box tracks delivery.

---

## v1 Requirements

### Determinism & Execution Substrate

> **Determinism is detected, not predicted.** There is deliberately no static
> analysis of task modules. Two nodes execute, their outputs are serialized and
> compared, and a mismatch is reported with the dissenting node named. Proving
> ahead of time that a module *cannot* diverge is a far harder problem than
> comparing two byte strings, and the comparison is the mechanism regardless —
> the cost of a nondeterministic module is one wasted redundant execution and a
> reported disagreement, which is what redundancy exists to surface.
>
> The sandbox needs no allow-list either: the host supplies four functions, and a
> module importing anything else fails at `WebAssembly.instantiate` with the
> offending import named. The runtime enforces it.

- [x] **DET-03**: Artifacts resolve only through a `key → CID` mapping signed by a
      trusted build authority, never by a bare CID — content addressing proves
      integrity, not provenance
- [x] **DET-05**: Anything hashed or content-addressed is encoded with strict
      DAG-CBOR — which rejects `NaN`/`Infinity`/`-Infinity`, forces `-0.0` to `+0.0`,
      and mandates one float width — and protobuf bytes are never hashed. Comparison is
      over the declared schema's encoded bytes, never a raw linear-memory slice
- [x] **DET-06**: A WASM task executes in a Web Worker with a narrow host ABI
      (no WASI clock, randomness, environment, or filesystem)
- [x] **DET-07**: The identical task-execution test suite passes under `node`,
      `browser`, and `webworker` targets

### Integrity & Verification

- [x] **VER-01**: A task can be dispatched to N independent executors and their
      outputs compared, with disagreement surfaced rather than silently resolved
- [x] **VER-02**: Executors commit to a result hash before revealing the result,
      so a replica cannot plagiarize a peer's answer
- [x] **VER-03**: No verification quorum rests on a single shared reachability
      dependency, so eclipsing a quorum requires compromising more than one of them.
      **Reworded 2026-08-03 by owner ruling; the property is unchanged and the bar is
      not lowered.** It used to read *"At least one replica of every verification
      quorum is anchored on a backbone node"*, and that phrasing encoded a
      **mechanism** — a node class — for a property that is about the **discovery
      graph**. The rationale clause was always the requirement: *eclipsing a quorum
      requires compromising more than one dependency.* `STATE.md` states the
      rule this collided with — *if a decision keys on node kind, it is wrong; the only
      legitimate use is shared-dependency analysis over the discovery graph* — and
      shared-dependency analysis is exactly what satisfies this. The old wording
      produced a real defect: Plan 19-02 implemented it as
      `discoverability === 'seed'`, which refused a quorum of relay-discovered peers
      even though Phase 3 had already measured one taking a verification slot (an
      iPhone at `/p2p-circuit/webrtc`, running half a 2×-redundant job). Retracted in
      `0314208`.
      **Why the browser tier does NOT close this, measured 2026-08-14 rather than assumed.**
      The plan of record was a `shard.quorum` renderer on the demo page, and that renderer now
      exists (region C22) and works — but `shared-relay-dependency` **cannot fire there**, for
      a reason that is the rule being right rather than a gap. In this fabric a relay
      *computes*: `fabric-node.ts`'s header records that the non-executing `RelayNode` class
      was deleted on purpose — *"all nodes have equal functionality, and the only difference is
      discovery"* — after a demo showed a relay *"present, connected, perfectly able to
      compute, and structurally excluded from doing so."* So the relay answers the offer probe,
      `main.ts`'s `computePeers()` counts it, and it is a candidate. It binds a socket, so it
      enrols `discoverability: 'seed'` with `relayIds: []`; `composeQuorum` orders candidates
      fewest-dependencies-first, so a seed is **always** in the member set; and `sharedRelay`
      returns `null` the moment a member is a seed, because a directly dialable node depends on
      no relay to be found. Measured directly: the fixture without the relay enrolled read
      `not attempted — this requestor holds no certificate for at least one candidate` and
      `3 node(s) computing — this tab and 2 peer(s)`, both explained by the same fact.
      `quorum-agents.node.test.ts` reaches rule 2 because `discoverCandidates` qualifies on
      **who advertises the input CID**, so its relay is not in the pool; the demo qualifies on
      **who answers an offer**, so its relay always is. **What would close this row** is a
      browser-tier candidate pool qualified by block advertisement rather than by liveness —
      a page change, not a test change — or an owner ruling that the node-tier reading suffices.
      Engineering the demo's pool to exclude the relay in a fixture would be shaping the
      evidence to reach a verdict, and was declined
      **SUPERSEDED 2026-08-14, recorded here 2026-08-18 — `shared-relay-dependency` DOES fire on
      the browser tier, and the paragraph above says it cannot.** The traceability table row for
      VER-03 has carried the close since `1931408`; this checkbox row was ticked in that same
      commit and its prose was left at the state it had four hours earlier, so the two halves of
      this file have disagreed about the same requirement ever since. Two claims above are false.
      First, *"`shared-relay-dependency` cannot fire there"*. Second, the remedy named as *"what
      would close this row"* — a candidate pool qualified by block advertisement — which was
      **refuted rather than built**: the input block is created inside `submitJob` *after* the
      pool is chosen, so nobody ever advertises it and the page would have gone permanently solo.
      What actually closed it is `QuorumRules.peerIdOf` (`7d5f0a7`, 16:07 against this prose's
      12:07). `relayIds` names relays by libp2p **peer id** while a quorum member is keyed by
      **`nodeKey`**, two spellings of one node that never compare equal — so the clause above,
      *"`sharedRelay` returns `null` the moment a member is a seed"*, was describing a **blind
      spot as though it were the rule being right**. Re-measured 2026-08-18 off the demo page on
      no flag, `quorum-ui.e2e.test.ts` 4/4 exit 0 in 31.15 s: `not composed
      [shared-relay-dependency] — relay 12D3KooWMZaFGYqsMBii5wJVVL3KhgchKVnYjBPXz83WvYRNP5UA is
      itself a member of the quorum and every other member is discoverable only through it`.
      Quoted rather than deleted, because it was true when written
- [x] **VER-04**: Quorum members are selected with anti-affinity, so one operator
      cannot supply a whole quorum
      *(**Closed 2026-08-14, and not by the change that was supposed to close it.** The
      2026-08-13 attempt landed the whole proposed reading in `705a716` and the box did not
      move, because the reading was behind `--discover`. This one is on the demo page's own
      Run button — **no flag** — so it escapes the ruling exactly as VER-10 did. Region C22
      renders `describeQuorum(shard.quorum)`, the composer's own words: a fabric of three
      distinct operators reads `composed across 2 operators (dockside-relay-co,
      quay-street-collective) — no two members share an operator`, and the identical fabric
      with every candidate under one operator reads `not composed [insufficient-operators] —
      quorum of 2 needs 2 distinct operators, found 1`. Two fabrics differing in `operatorId`
      alone, two different strings, asserted unequal so neither can be a constant —
      `quorum-ui.e2e.test.ts`, exit 0. Planted red: the region pinned to a fixed composed
      sentence fails all three cases, `PLANTED_EXIT=1`, restored by surgical inverse with
      `cmp` exit 0 and sha256 back to `b20ca7d4`.
      **CORRECTED 2026-08-18 — the fabric named above is the wrong one, and the sentence was
      falsified four hours after it was written.** It reads *"a fabric of three distinct
      operators reads `composed across 2 operators (dockside-relay-co, quay-street-collective)`"*.
      That fabric is the file's **distinct-operators** arm, it runs on **one** relay, and
      `7d5f0a7` — `QuorumRules.peerIdOf`, landed 16:07 the same day against this prose's 12:07 —
      makes rule 2 see the relay as a member, so it now reads `not composed
      [shared-relay-dependency]`. **The box does not move**, because an unflagged composed reading
      still exists: it comes from a **third arm** added at `ebf6932` that stands the two tabs on
      **two** independently-run relays, four distinct operators. Re-measured 2026-08-18 off the
      demo page, `quorum-ui.e2e.test.ts` 4/4 exit 0 in 31.15 s: `composed across 2 operators
      (harbour-line-signals, dockside-relay-co) — no two members share an operator`. So *"two
      fabrics differing in `operatorId` alone"* is wrong too — there are **three** arms, and the
      composed one differs by a whole second relay. That is the only thing that *can* differ:
      **no arrangement of operators over a one-relay fabric composes**, because rule 2 is not a
      rule about operators — the single relay is the single point of failure of everything it
      carries and is itself a member. The old sentences are quoted rather than deleted because
      they were true when written.)*
- [x] **VER-05**: The verifier compares `(task, outputs)` only — timing, fuel, and
      node metadata sit outside the signed digest
- [x] **VER-06**: Redundancy factor is a per-job dial reaching 1 (off), and the
      verification tax is reported as a measured cost on every job
- [x] **VER-08**: When an owner has two or more live nodes, a sovereignty-pinned
      task executes redundantly across the owner's own node set and the outputs
      are compared — no data leaves the owner's trust domain
- [x] **VER-09**: When an owner has fewer than two live nodes, the task executes
      once and the receipt records it as owner-attested rather than verified
      *(**Still open on 2026-08-18, and the row's own statement of what it needed was
      the first of two blockers rather than the whole of it.** The descriptor half is
      now built — `demo/main.ts`'s candidate lookup carries a certified owner id — and
      it is not enough. Measured off `demo-byo.e2e.test.ts`'s `[sovereign·placed]` arm
      the same day: every sovereign dispatch this page makes is refused at the executor,
      `unauthorized: no pinned owner key for public on this node` on each peer tab and
      `sovereignty violation: node … is not cleared to execute sovereign data` on the
      submitting one. No sovereign task has ever executed from this surface, so there is
      no receipt for the page to render `owner-attested` off.)*
      *(**Closed 2026-08-19 on the NODE tier, and the route is a change of route rather than
      a change of standard.** What the row asked for is quoted exactly: *"a default path that
      reaches an owner-pinned execution at fewer than two live owner nodes and renders
      `owner-attested` off it."* `bin/agent.ts`'s sovereign coordinator leg is one — two
      owners, one node each, each shard executed once at `redundancy: 1` because pinning
      removes the second independent executor, and the receipt printed through the same
      `attestation.description` the demo's receipt panel renders. The sentence above that
      named `demo-byo.e2e.test.ts` as *the* checkable statement of done was written on
      2026-08-18 and is **superseded rather than deleted**: it picked the page because the
      page looked like the only surface with no flag in front of it, and the owner's
      role-selector refinement — ruled the same day, at
      `.planning/consults/2026-08-18-owner-ruling-role-selector-vs-feature-gate.md` — makes an
      agent flag that names *whose data this process acts for* a second such surface. **The
      browser leg stays open and is not folded into this tick**; it is recorded in this row
      and in `MR-02`'s as what did not arrive.)*
      *(**Closed 2026-08-11.** The clause this row was held open for was named rather than
      inferred — both readings that existed were of a *public* job at `redundancy: 1`, while
      this requirement is about the sovereign path. `bin/bench.ts --discover --sovereign`
      now prints the receipt for its owner-pinned shard, and the line is read off the
      spawned binary in `sovereign-arm.node.test.ts`.)*
- [x] **VER-10**: Owner-domain quorum agreement is reported as a distinct,
      weaker claim than independent-operator agreement, so the stronger guarantee
      is never implied by the weaker one

### Data, Content Addressing & Sovereignty

- [x] **DATA-01**: Task inputs, outputs, and intermediates are content-addressed
      and retrievable by CID
- [x] **DATA-02**: A blockstore adapter works against IndexedDB in the browser and
      the filesystem in Node behind one interface
- [x] **DATA-03**: Data carries a sovereignty label that travels with it and acts
      as a hard scheduling constraint
- [x] **DATA-04**: A sovereignty-pinned task executes only within the owner's own
      node set — the scheduler cannot relocate it outside that set to balance load
- [x] **DATA-05**: The stream tap refuses to send a frame carrying registered
      sovereign data, so the bytes never leave the node, and the running job fails
      as a consequence
      <!-- Restated 2026-07-28 against the amended ROADMAP criterion 1, and true of
      the code as of Plans 13-04, 13-05 and 13-07.

      The refusal. `EgressGuard.send` computes the match, pushes the entry carrying
      its violation label, and then rejects with `EgressRefusal` instead of calling
      the inner transport — the frame is never handed to the wire. Push-before-throw
      means stopping a leak never costs the record that it was stopped, and
      `manifest.totalBytes` sums only entries with no violation, so a manifest
      holding one refused frame reads `entries.length === 1` with `totalBytes === 0`.
      A node that refused and a node that sent nothing are not confusable (13-04).

      The granularity, in the form it was measured rather than the form it was
      wished for. A match is the *whole registered payload, contiguous and
      byte-identical*. A re-encoded, compressed or partial copy is not matched, and a
      probe sending the raw field characters alone — eleven bytes — crossed with no
      violation. Phase 4 drew the same line: a detector, not a prover.

      What the caller observes, and the asymmetry, which is an accepted cost rather
      than an open defect. On a node's own outbound request the refusal arrives
      immediately as a named send failure carrying the violated label. On a reply the
      failure is swallowed by `rpc.ts`'s documented response-leg behavior, so the
      dispatcher waits out its own timeout and learns that the dispatch failed but
      not why. Closing that would change every peer's response-leg failure semantics
      to fix legibility rather than correctness, and the evidence for the cause lives
      in the owner's own manifest — where sovereignty says it belongs.

      A registration lives only until its reply frame has settled (13-07), so the
      scan the refusal depends on is bounded by a node's in-flight sovereign tasks
      rather than by its uptime.

      Proven in-process against real `FabricNode`s and across two spawned
      `bin/agent.ts` processes (13-05). -->
- [x] **DATA-06**: Every job emits an egress manifest recording exactly what left
      the submitting node, with byte counts, retrievable from the job's own result
      <!-- Re-checked 2026-07-28 against the amended ROADMAP criterion 2, which
      narrowed the promise from "each owner's node" to the *submitting* node's own
      tap. That is what `submitJobWithEgress` delivers: the manifest comes back on
      the job's own result value, sliced per job so two jobs against one guard do not
      double-count (13-01). The browser demo's two real Chromium e2e tests read it
      back from the `window.o2` API's own return value rather than from a guard a
      harness built, and 13-VERIFICATION.md mutation-verified that leg
      independently. `bin/bench.ts`'s two fabrics do the same from a runnable entry
      point, and 13-06 puts a test behind those call sites rather than leaving the
      type-checker as their only guard.

      What the amendment gave up, stated so nobody re-derives it. Reading a *remote*
      node's manifest needs a wire message kind that does not exist — `protocol.ts`
      carries exec, block, providers, records, offer, reservations, report and error
      and nothing else — `13-CONTEXT.md` deferred building one, and the criterion
      went on promising it anyway. Cross-process retrieval is now a named future item
      rather than an implied promise, and the "each owner's node" clause is gone
      rather than quietly reinterpreted.

      `bin/agent.ts` is outside this requirement's scope: it is serving-only, 71
      lines, and never calls `submitJob`, so it has no job of its own to emit a
      manifest against.

      **CORRECTED 2026-08-22** — that sentence was true of the file as it stood on
      2026-07-28 and has been false since 2026-08-19. `bin/agent.ts` is 2610 lines and
      submits jobs of its own on two legs — `--coordinate` and the sovereign combine —
      each through `submitJobWithEgress` with `[node.egress]` as the guard list, and each
      rendering the manifest off the job's own result. It is inside this requirement's
      scope and it satisfies it. One clause survives, and it is the literal one: the
      binary still calls no *bare* `submitJob`, a set `sovereign-block-refusal.node.test.ts`
      pins at three files with this one absent, and `bin/agent.ts` states the rule in its
      own words beside the call. What was false was the conjunction — serving-only,
      71 lines, no job of its own — which is how a sentence that names three facts stays
      persuasive after two of them expire. -->

<!-- Both rows above were marked done on their executors' reports. The first
independent pass scored the phase 0/3 fully verified. Re-check the ledger against the
code, not the reports. -->

- [x] **DATA-07**: Filters, projections, and partial aggregation push down to the
      owner's node so the least data leaves
- [x] **DATA-08**: Artifact `key → CID` mappings are signed by a trusted build

- [x] **DATA-09**: Backbone encrypted replicas serve availability only and are

### Authorization & Node Identity

- [x] **AUTH-01**: A node's identity key is generated on-device and its public half
      is signed into a provider-issued certificate
- [x] **AUTH-02**: A node verifies a peer's provider-signed certificate offline,
      with no live certificate authority
- [x] **AUTH-03**: A task carries a delegatable, expiry-scoped capability chain
      rooted at the data owner's key, verified before execution
      *(**Closed 2026-08-19**, on the clause this row had written down for itself rather
      than on a widening of what counts. It read: what is missing is *"a no-flag production
      dispatch that hands a real `CapabilitySupplier` to a `RemoteExecutor` placed on a node
      whose descriptor `ownerId` equals the shard's"*. `bin/agent.ts`'s sovereign
      coordinator leg is that dispatch — see the traceability row for the reading and for
      why its flags are role selectors rather than feature gates under the owner's ruling of
      2026-08-18.)*
- [x] **AUTH-04**: Enrollment is provider-gated and rate-limited, so mass fake-node
      creation is costly
- [x] **AUTH-05**: Multiple node identity certificates chain to a single owner's
      user key, forming a discoverable replica set that the scheduler can target

### Transport, Discovery & Connectivity

- [x] **NET-01**: Two Node.js processes discover each other and exchange tasks over
      a real network transport
- [x] **NET-02**: Two browser tabs connect to each other via WebRTC using a
      Circuit Relay v2 peer for SDP signaling
- [ ] **NET-03**: A backbone relay node auto-acquires a TLS certificate and accepts
      browser reservations without manual certificate management.
      **The acquisition clause was measured 2026-08-17 and the blocker that held it was
      misattributed.** It read *"AutoTLS needs a publicly reachable host with a real DNS
      name"* — which is a fact about **Let's Encrypt**, not about the mechanism. A public
      authority will not name a domain you cannot demonstrate control of; the acquisition
      path itself needs a certificate authority and nothing else. `local-acme.ts` is one:
      RFC 8555 on loopback with every JWS verified against the account's own RSA key, a UDP
      DNS zone the authority genuinely queries for the dns-01 record, and a p2p-forge
      stand-in that authenticates the caller through libp2p's own PeerID-auth handshake
      before placing anything. `@ipshipyard/libp2p-auto-tls` runs against it unmodified and
      `auto-tls.node.test.ts` reads seven cases: a certificate ordered, issued and installed
      with none configured anywhere; a TLS handshake to the socket the node already held,
      validating the chain under the name AutoTLS derived from the peer id; the published
      `/tls/sni/<name>/ws` address; a restart that reuses the stored certificate and places
      **one** order across two starts; and two negatives that fail at different stages.
      The dns-01 check was proved load-bearing by planting `if (true)` over it and watching
      the wrong-record case go red at `expect(acme.issued().length).toBe(0)`.
      **Two findings the code now carries**: libp2p's `announce` *suppresses* the certified
      address, because `address-manager` returns the announce list and returns early without
      reaching the DNS-mapping fold — so the option is `appendAnnounce`; and the certified
      name is published as an **SNI component inside an existing `/tls` address**, never as
      a `/dns4` address of its own.
      **The box stays unchecked, and what is left is hosting rather than code.** The
      authority is not Let's Encrypt, and the node's dialable address is *declared* rather
      than bound because every interface on this host is RFC 1918 or a ULA — measured, not
      assumed. Whether a public CA would issue is a deployment question this repository
      cannot answer from a laptop, and an unchecked box here understates on purpose
- [x] **NET-04**: Relayed protocol handlers register with `runOnLimitedConnection`,
      and no hardcoded certhash multiaddr is required to join
- [x] **NET-05**: Relay reservation exhaustion is detected and reported rather than
      failing silently
- [x] **NET-06**: Browser peers participate in routing as full peers. Backbone nodes
      may serve records on their behalf as an optimisation and as a fallback when a
      browser holds no relay reservation — not because a browser is incapable.
      **Revised 2026-07-26** (owner decision): a browser peer differs from a backbone
      peer only in that it cannot bind a listening socket. Holding a relay
      reservation makes it dialable, which was demonstrated in Phase 3 when an iPhone
      was dialled at its `/p2p-circuit/webrtc` address and ran half of a 2×-redundant
      job. Client-mode-only was an assumption inherited from research, not a measured
      limit
- [x] **NET-07**: A constants-regression test asserts the relay and transport
      limits the system depends on, failing CI when an upgrade changes them

- [x] **SCHED-03**: An over-committed node rejects work and the requestor re-picks

      ***Extended 2026-08-18 — the box does not move and the requirement's own sentence

      onward and nothing branched on it: reasons are strings and this repository does not
      switch control flow on a reason, so `planWithOffers` re-offered a stood-out node
      every remaining shard — and preferred it, because a paused node correctly publishes

      pieces now exist. `OfferStanding` is a required field on `Admission`'s refusal arm
      and on the offer frame, and `planWithOffers` keeps a `stoodDown` set that is

### Map / Reduce

- [x] **MR-01**: A job partitions into N shards, each executing independently, with
      a partition index and count available to the task
- [x] **MR-02**: Each owner computes a local partial over its own data with no
      map-side data movement
      *(**Still open on 2026-08-18.** This row asked for *"a surface able to hand a tab
      or an agent a real owner identity rather than the `public` every descriptor
      currently declares"*, and that surface now exists — but it does not close the row,
      because the map it would enable is refused at the executor for a second reason the
      row never named. See VER-09 above for the measurement, which is the same one.)*
      *(**Closed 2026-08-19 on the AGENT, which is the half of that sentence this row
      could reach.** The clause above says *"a tab **or an agent**"*, and it is the agent
      that got one: `bin/agent.ts --sovereign-owner/--sovereign-row` places each owner's
      row on that owner's own process against a descriptor whose `ownerId` came off a
      certificate, and then aggregates the two partials at two replicas per combine —
      `reduceSovereignJob`'s second production call site, and its first that no flag
      stands in front of. The tab half is unchanged and is **not** claimed: every
      sovereign dispatch from the demo page is still refused, for the reason `VER-09`'s
      row records, and closing it needs node-operator configuration `tab-api.ts`
      deliberately keeps off the page's contract.
      **SUPERSEDED 2026-08-20 — the tab half closed too, and by enrolment rather than by
      configuration.** Both clauses above were true on 2026-08-19 and were falsified the
      next day. `demo/main.ts`'s `sovereignChainsFor` mints a chain rooted at the visitor's
      own non-extractable key and hands it to every executor on the BYO dispatch path, over
      the listed and the discovered populations alike; `enrolledUserKey` derives the node's
      `sovereignty` from this origin's own stored certificate. So `TabApi.start` still grows
      no `sovereignty` parameter — that part of the old sentence remains true — and none was
      needed, which is the part that was wrong. `owner-domain-tabs.e2e.test.ts` places an
      owner-pinned shard submitted from the page onto two peer tabs of one profile, reads
      the owner id back off the page's own candidate list rather than being handed it,
      asserts the refusals do not contain `no capability chain supplied`, and reads
      `owner-domain` in the attestation region with neither `independent` nor
      `owner-attested` present. What is still refused is narrower than "every sovereign
      dispatch" and belongs on the record: a dispatch naming an owner id this tab cannot
      sign for, and any dispatch from a tab that has never enrolled. What this row still
      does **not** claim is a sovereign *aggregation* from a tab — `reduceSovereignJob` has
      no browser-tier caller.)*
- [x] **MR-03**: Partials merge up a hierarchical tree via an associative,
      commutative combine
- [x] **MR-04**: The reduce tree is derived deterministically from the sorted
      contribution set — contributor id first, the partial CID breaking ties — so
      the same contributions always yield a byte-identical tree with no consensus
      step
      *(**REWORDED BY OWNER RULING, 2026-08-18. This is a NARROWING, and it is written
      here as one.** The original is quoted in full so the change of subject is
      auditable rather than merely recorded: *"The reduce tree is derived
      deterministically from sorted partial CIDs, so every participant computes an
      identical tree with no consensus"*. Two of its clauses were false of the code and
      one names an experiment that cannot be run as the system is architected.
      **(a) The sort was never on partial CIDs.** `deriveReduceTree` sorts on `leafId`,
      which is the contributor id, a NUL byte, and the CID
      (`packages/core/src/reduce.ts:180`), so the contributor string decides and the CID
      only breaks a tie — a factual correction, not a relaxation, and the reworded
      sentence says which key it is.
      **(b) *"every participant"* is unwritable here, and that is a measurement rather
      than an opinion.** `deriveReduceTree` is declared in `packages/core/src/reduce.ts` and has
      exactly **one** production call site — the `deriveReduceTree(` call inside `reduceJob`
      (`packages/net/src/reduce-job.ts`) — which is inside the
      requestor; the projection closure never crosses a wire, so no second participant
      holds the contribution set to derive from and cannot compute even one leaf id. That
      claim is not left as prose: the traceability row states it in the shape
      `requirements-ledger.node.test.ts` parses — *`deriveReduceTree` is reachable only
      through `reduceJob`* — so a second call site anywhere in production turns the ledger
      guard red rather than quietly making this note stale.
      **(c) What the ruling kept.** Under the owner's ruling of 2026-08-14
      (`.planning/consults/2026-08-14-owner-ruling-sovereign-threat-model.md`) the
      property that sentence protects is **routing stability and dedup**, not fraud
      prevention — the requestor of a sovereign job is the data owner, so a requestor
      lying about its own tree is an attack on its own job. What survives, and what the
      reworded text now says, is that the derivation is a pure function of the
      contribution set. **This is strictly weaker than what it replaces**, and no reader
      should take the tick as saying a second participant has derived anything.
      **(d) Measured, not asserted.** `packages/core/src/reduce.test.ts`'s *"MR-04 as
      reworded"* block derives the tree twice from identical inputs — the second from
      CIDs rebuilt out of their string form, so object identity cannot carry it — and
      compares a positional serialisation of every field, with an anti-vacuity arm
      requiring one changed contribution to differ; and reads the ordering key in both
      directions, contributor id primary and CID as tie-break only.)*
- [x] **MR-05**: Combine executors are assigned by rendezvous hashing, yielding a
      ranked fallback list
- [x] **MR-06**: A combine lost to churn is recomputed elsewhere from its
      content-addressed inputs, with no state migration
      *(**Closed 2026-08-18, on the requirement's own sentence rather than on a widening of
      it, and the row it closes against had been holding itself open on a clause this
      requirement does not contain.** The evidence is
      `packages/node/src/tree-reduce-agents.node.test.ts` —
      `describe('MR-05 / MR-06 — a combine node SIGKILLed mid-reduce is repaired elsewhere')`
      — across **eight real spawned `bin/agent.ts` processes**, not a fixture and not a skip:
      re-run 2026-08-18, `--project node`, **EXIT=0, 5 passed, `real 12.56` / `user 56.30`**,
      no `skipIf` anywhere in the file. The victim is the rendezvous-rank-0 node of the first
      level-1 combine, `SIGKILL`ed mid-flight and asserted dead by `signalCode`, with a
      **positive control** proving it owned that combine on the healthy run — so
      `executedBy.get(node0) !== victimId` is a repair rather than a coincidence. *No state
      migration* is read from both ends: the combine frame carries exactly four keys and its
      inputs are CIDs, and the replacement process is shown holding every input while having
      **received none of them — it asked for each by CID**. And the whole reduce is re-run
      through the production `reduceJob` entry point with the node already dead.
      **What was holding the box was a demo-surface clause, and it is retained rather than
      retired below** — nothing on the page loses a combine to churn. That is true, it is
      worth having written down, and it is **not** what MR-06 asks: the requirement names a
      combine, its content-addressed inputs and the absence of state migration, and says
      nothing about a browser. `reduceJob` is reached with no flag from the page's own Run
      button, so the mechanism is on a default path and only the *fault* is the test's to
      inject — which is the one thing no operator can be asked to supply.)*
- [x] **MR-07**: A duplicate combine result is discarded harmlessly because it

### Benchmark & Proof

- [x] **BENCH-01**: A benchmark harness measures job makespan against participating
      node count, reproducibly
- [x] **BENCH-02**: Benchmark methodology is pre-registered and committed before
      the first published number
- [x] **BENCH-03**: Published results report p99 makespan, not mean
- [x] **BENCH-04**: The verification tax is included in reported cost, never
      excluded
- [x] **BENCH-05**: A COST crossover is published — the node count at which the
      system beats a competent single-threaded implementation
- [ ] **BENCH-06**: Benchmarks run across N independent operating-system processes on one
      host; every published run records its machine inventory; and the same-machine label
      stays **required and derived from that inventory**, never declared — sixteen nodes on
      one laptop are reported as sixteen processes on one machine, not as sixteen nodes.
      **The distinct-machine claim is descoped, not satisfied.** It is unmeasured, and
      unmeasured is not met. A same-host run **cannot** detect divergence between machines,
      because it has one CPU, one V8 and one libc: every process shares an instruction set,
      an engine build and a system library, so the very variables a cross-machine benchmark
      exists to expose are held constant by construction. The original cross-machine risk
      stands exactly where Phase 8 left it, unmeasured and now unscheduled. *(**`unscheduled`
      is stale — corrected 2026-08-25 by a hand re-read of this row.** It is scheduled:
      **Phase 39 criterion 4** is its named closer, and this file's own v2.0 carried-ids table
      already said so while this sentence went on denying it. `unmeasured` stands and is the
      whole of what holds the row open.)* What this
      rewrite *does* buy is real and is tracked separately as **BENCH-07** (Phase 23, the
      multi-process benchmark driver): moving off one event loop makes a parallel speedup
      measurable at all, which the single-process harness could not do at any N. That is the
      honest one-machine win. It is not a distributed measurement and must never be
      published as one

      row rather than minting a fresh one here. The machines half is still unchanged and

- [x] **DEMO-02**: The demo runs a task a person cares about, not a synthetic
      protocol exercise
      *(**Widened 2026-08-10 by Phase 27**: two workloads a visitor can press — a
      Pythagorean-triple 2-colouring search, and a π estimate merged through a verified

      took UI-SPEC section 10's Option A, `PRIMES_RECORD` was signed and `window.o2.runPrimes`

- [x] **DEMO-03**: The demo is deployable to static hosting with no server-side
      process beyond the backbone relay
- [x] **DEMO-04**: Public deployment is an explicitly triggered action, never an
      automatic consequence of a phase completing.
      **REPURPOSED by owner ruling 2026-08-25 — the mechanism stands, the reason changed.**
      The row was written to prevent an irreversible legal event: publishing forfeits EPO and

      completing. **That reason is spent twice over** — the repository has been public since

      unchanged above because the sentence is still true; what is retired is only its
      *rationale*, and the replacement is narrower and cheaper to check: **deploying a paid
      tier does not happen by itself.** In a milestone whose whole point is to add a

      hard spending ceiling, so an accidental deploy has no floor under it. Do NOT read this as

### Native → WASM AOT (Part I)

- [x] **AOT-01**: A statically-linked AArch64 binary translates to a `.wasm`
      artifact via the elfconv pipeline — and the driver refuses to trust the
      toolchain's exit code, which is `0` on a binary leaving 174 addresses
      untranslated. Verdict `reservations`, exit 2
- [x] **AOT-02**: Translated artifacts are content-addressed with a cache key
      covering input digest, toolchain versions, target, and WASM feature set;
      pinned to a hardcoded conformance CID, and the image is keyed by digest so a
      re-tagged local image is refused rather than hashed under a borrowed name.
      **The re-tag clause was carried as a measured negative from 2026-08-04 to
      2026-08-18, and it is now MET.** What this row said, kept because every clause of
      it about `RepoDigests` re-measures true: *"measured 2026-08-04 on Docker Server
      29.4.0: `docker tag` copies the origin's manifest digest into a `RepoDigests` entry
      for the borrowed repository, and because `RepoDigests` belongs to the image ID
      rather than to the reference inspected, the canonical name then answers with the
      identical list. No predicate over that list can separate the two, and requiring
      every entry to agree was measured to refuse the canonical image — a false refusal
      in place of a merely unportable name."* All of that still holds. **What is false is
      that `RepoDigests` was the whole data source.** `docker image inspect` also reports
      `Identity`, which records where content was *obtained* rather than what it has been
      *named*, and measured 2026-08-18 on the same host `docker tag` does **not** move it:
      both names answer `{"Pull":[{"Repository":"ghcr.io/yomaytk/elfconv"}]}`. The
      predicate is over that data **and the name asked for** — the requested repository
      must be among the repositories the content was pulled under — which refuses the
      borrowed call and admits the canonical one from one set of bytes. Proved through the
      spawned CLI against a real `docker tag` of the real image: `EXIT=1`, *"never pulled
      anything under docker.io/o2-local/elfconv"*, and the old adoption line
      `image o2-local/elfconv@sha256:` asserted absent. Run against all 16 images on the
      host: **zero false refusals**. Two plants watched red, restored by surgical inverse,
      `cmp` exit 0. See the 2026-08-18 AMENDMENT in `21-VERIFICATION.md`.
      **The classic dockerd store is still unmeasured, and unmeasured is not met** — a
      daemon reporting no `Identity` is admitted with `IMAGE_PROVENANCE_BLIND_SPOT`
      attached rather than refused, because refusing there is the false-refusal trade
      that was already rejected.
      **The box stayed `[ ]` until 2026-08-18 for one remaining reason, and the sentence
      is quoted rather than deleted because a reader has to be able to see what was
      closed:** *"`describeKey` is reachable only through `describeLift`, so the mismatch
      report its own docblock names as the second reason it exists still does not exist."*
      **Both halves of that are now false, by build rather than by rewording.** The owner
      decided on 2026-08-18 that the expected key comes from **the signed artifact record**
      — over a caller-supplied pin and over dropping the report — so `NameRecord` gained an
      optional `translationKeyCid` **inside its signature**, `--publish-as` writes it,
      `--against-record` reads it, and `tools/aot/lift.ts`'s `vouchedTranslation` is the
      second caller of `describeKey`. A lift that produces a different key than the record
      vouches for is refused by name with both keys named and this side's key rendered;
      a record carrying no key at all is a separate refusal, because *there is nothing to
      compare against* and *these are two different translations* are different statements.
      **The limit travels with the claim**: the record carries the publisher's key by CID
      and not by field — `TranslationKey` lives in `@o2/aot`, which depends on `@o2/core` —
      so the report names both keys and can render only the one it computed. The
      field-level diff scoped at 35–55 lines in the traceability row was **not** built and
      is not claimed. See that row for which clause each piece of evidence covers.
- [ ] **AOT-03**: Translation is reproducible on one host — repeated lifts of identical
      input bytes, each running the toolchain in its own freshly spawned container process
      on this machine, produce byte-identical artifacts and therefore an identical CID.
      Measured: two lifts minutes apart, `sha256 490eeed5…`, same `inputDigest`, same
      toolchain versions, same feature set.
      **The cross-machine claim is descoped, not satisfied.** It is unmeasured, and
      unmeasured is not met — two lifts on two hosts have still never been compared.
      **The local shortcut was tried on 2026-08-17 and is measured closed.** This host can
      run two machine architectures: elfconv publishes `:arm64` and `:amd64`, both are
      present, and `uname -m` inside them reports `aarch64` and `x86_64`. Swapping the image
      does not move one translator to a second machine — it swaps the translator.
      `backend/remill/lib/Arch/Arch.cpp` gates each front end behind its own `#if`
      (`ELFCONV_AARCH64_BUILD`, `ELFCONV_X86_BUILD`), no build defines both, and the x86_64
      image handed this repository's AArch64 fixture reaches the `default:` arm and calls
      `LOG(FATAL)`: `Arch.cpp:211] OS: 2 ArchName: 8`, exit 134, in 18.5 s — against the
      arm64 image's clean lift to `sha256:8dcf62e1…` in 368 s. No second artifact exists, so
      nothing about reproducibility was learned, and **what the row needs is a second
      `aarch64` Linux host running the same elfconv build.** Guarded by
      `tools/aot/cross-machine.node.test.ts`, which goes red if a build ever defines both
      flags and reopens the comparison.
      **`CROSS_MACHINE_BLIND_SPOT` stays attached to every artifact and stays printed by
      the CLI.** Phase 10 established that the blind spot is **structural, not
      configurational**: elfconv's virtual-register promotion iterates a pointer-keyed
      `std::unordered_map` and a `std::set<BBBag*>`, whose iteration order is an
      address-space property, so no flag, no version pin and no image digest removes it.
      Descoping this requirement removes neither the marker nor the risk it names, and the
      `never claims cross-machine reproducibility` guard in `tools/aot/lift.node.test.ts`
      stays in force.
      *(Status: the one-host half is established. The box stays unchecked because the
      descoped cross-machine half is carried as unmeasured rather than as met — an
      unchecked box here understates on purpose, which is the safe direction.)*
- [x] **AOT-04**: A translated artifact executes on the fabric under the same
      admission checks and verification as a source-compiled one — proved through
      the `@o2/aot` barrel, and the ABI verified against a real elfconv artifact
      rather than against fixtures written to match the assumption
      *(**Both named clauses are satisfied and measured as of 2026-08-11** — see the
      traceability row, which was stale about the second. The box stayed `[ ]` for a clause
      the row states rather than the requirement does: no browser tab had ever executed a
      translated artifact, so "the fabric" was established over one of its two tiers.
      **That clause is closed as of 2026-08-16** — `packages/node/src/aot-tab.e2e.test.ts`
      runs a lifted artifact inside a live Chromium tab through `BrowserNode`'s composed
      chain. The box stays `[ ]` one more beat because both tiers' readings are gated on a
      gitignored artifact and the browser one is a single engine; ticking is now a decision
      to take against the row, not a measurement still owed.*
      ***Both tiers independently re-measured 2026-08-18*** *by the pass that closed AOT-02's
      re-tag clause, so the decision is now being offered against a fresh reading rather than
      against a citation:* `npx vitest run --project node packages/node/src/aot-dispatch.node.test.ts`
      → **EXIT=0, 3 passed**, `grossFuel=208 usefulFuel=104 multiplier=2` and
      `wire: 5660003 bytes, 150ms, fetched=true, ok=true, dir grew 3→5` — the fuel arithmetic
      reproduces exactly and the wire figure sits inside 21-05's recorded `158 / 177 / 689 ms`
      band; and `npx vitest run --project e2e packages/node/src/aot-tab.e2e.test.ts` →
      **EXIT=0, 2 passed**, neither file skipped. *Nothing in this row is outstanding as a
      measurement.* **The box moved on 2026-08-18, and the three edits that pass named as its
      price were taken together in one commit** — this file's header counts, the traceability

      `packages/node/src/requirements-ledger.node.test.ts`, whose `REREAD_REGISTER_CEILING`

      *"The box is still `[ ]`, and that is a scope refusal rather than a judgment"*, naming
      the header sentences and the register entry as what ticking also required and observing
      that ticking without both leaves the tree red. That was correct, and it is what the
      ticking commit did.
      **Both tiers were re-measured again on the day the box moved rather than carried across
      from the paragraph above:** `--project node packages/node/src/aot-dispatch.node.test.ts`
      → **EXIT=0, 3 passed, `real 5.94`**, and `--project e2e packages/node/src/aot-tab.e2e.test.ts`
      → **EXIT=0, 2 passed, `real 3.21`**. Neither run skipped a case, and the gitignored
      fixtures both files are `skipIf`-gated on are present on this host.
      **The two conditions this row was held open for are recorded rather than retired, because
      each is a condition on the EVIDENCE and not on the mechanism:** both readings are gated on
      a gitignored lifted artifact, so a fresh clone measures nothing here until it lifts one at
      ~100 s per guest; and the browser reading is **one** engine, Chromium via Playwright, not
      the three-engine matrix the `browser` project runs. A reader who holds that a single-engine
      browser reading is not "the fabric" should clear this box rather than assume the condition
      was overlooked.)*
- [x] **AOT-05**: Browser artifact loading uses `compileStreaming` against a stable
      gateway URL so V8 code caching applies. **Loading done; caching does not
      happen.** No WASM code-cache entry at 4.8 MB over three visits, while the
      same profile caches 2 MB of JavaScript. Published as a measured negative
      with two controls, not deferred
      *(REFUTED 2026-08-14 — the negative above is quoted rather than deleted, because what
      was wrong with it is the instructive part. V8's WASM code cache is written and read
      back on this tree: `code-cache.e2e.test.ts` asserts a `wasm.SerializeModule` write and
      an on-disk entry above 4096 B on the first visit, and `wasm.GetNativeModuleFromCache`,
      `wasm.Deserialize` and `wasm.CompilationAfterDeserialization` on the second — through
      the raw platform API and through the shipped `loadArtifact` alike. The old reading was
      a property of the fixture rather than of the engine: `syntheticArtifact`'s function
      bodies called nothing, so they never produced enough top-tier code to reach
      `--wasm-caching-threshold`, and the 4.8 MB straight-line shape is kept in the same run
      and the same apparatus as a **control**, not as the finding. The "two controls" this
      row claims were one observation and a constant — `CACHE_TRACE_NAMES` listed four trace
      names that never fire on this path, so the trace leg reported "none" on a real hit. The
      residual has since been sited on V8's quiescence window, `--wasm-caching-timeout-ms`.
      This row's traceability cell carries the working.)*
