| AOT-02 | Phase 21 — AOT Translation Signing & Runtime | **Done** — this row previously said the lift pipeline made no such call and built no record, and both halves of that became false on 2026-08-04 (Plan 21-01). Paraphrased rather than quoted, because a row that reproduces the sentence it is correcting has that sentence read back as its own claim. `tools/aot/lift.ts` calls `translationCid` on every successful lift, `LiftedArtifact.translation` is a **required** field so no lift returns bytes without a `TranslationRecord` over input digest, toolchain versions, target and feature set, and a lift the key refuses is a named failure — `unnameable` — rather than a success with `'unknown'` hashed into its identity. Coverage runs both ways with no container: six flips move the emitted CID and nine fields leave it still, and same-host repeatability now asserts the emitted CID rather than only its three ingredients. **Two of the three reasons this row gave for not being Done were retired on 2026-08-04, and are paraphrased rather than quoted for the reason given above.** The display leg: `describeKey` now has a production caller — `describeLift` renders the key as hashed on a `key as hashed:` line, which is also the only line carrying `inputDigest`, the one key field nothing else printed. The argv leg: `--image` and `--docker` are on the CLI and reach the driver (Plan 21-02). **Outstanding, and the reason this is still not Done:** `describeKey` is reachable only through `describeLift`, so the key renders on the build path and nowhere else — the *mismatch report* its own docblock names as the second reason it exists does not exist. **The clause that used to follow that one is retired, corrected 2026-08-05.** It read *“and correspondingly the emitted CID has never been compared across two genuinely different inputs end to end (Plan 21-04)”*; Plan 21-04 did exactly that — two real lifts, two different printed key CIDs, with the moved input named — and `21-VERIFICATION.md` scored that clause MET. The row's checkable claim is untouched and was re-derived by symbol here: nothing outside `describeLift` calls `describeKey`. **The re-tag clause was a measured negative from 2026-08-04 and is MET as of 2026-08-18.** The sentence this row carried is kept because it is still true of what it measured: *"`docker tag` gives the borrowed repository a `RepoDigests` entry of its own carrying the origin's manifest digest, so the repository match succeeds and the borrowed name is adopted rather than refused. `RepoDigests` is a property of the image **ID**, not of the reference inspected, so once a borrowed tag exists the canonical name returns a byte-identical list — **no predicate over that list can tell the two calls apart**, and requiring every entry to agree was measured to refuse the *canonical* image, trading an unportable name for a false refusal."* Re-measured 2026-08-18 and every clause of it reproduced. **The false part is the unstated premise that `RepoDigests` was the data source.** `docker image inspect` also reports `Identity` — where the content was *obtained*, not what it has been *named* — and `docker tag` leaves it unmoved through either name (`{"Pull":[{"Repository":"ghcr.io/yomaytk/elfconv"}]}` before and after, measured). `resolveImage` now refuses any `--image` whose requested repository is not among the repositories the content was pulled under, normalising the reference the way Docker does because `Identity` spells repositories fully qualified while `RepoDigests` spells them short. Proved through the spawned program against a real `docker tag` of the real elfconv image: `EXIT=1` with a named refusal, and the old adoption line `image o2-local/elfconv@sha256:` — the string `21-VERIFICATION.md` quoted as *"that sentence **is** the criterion's failure"* — asserted absent. Zero false refusals across all 16 images on the host; un-normalised, 9 of 11 pulled images would be falsely refused, which is what the second plant holds. Two plants watched red and restored by surgical inverse with `cmp` exit 0. The classic dockerd image store is still unmeasured, and unmeasured is not met: a daemon reporting no `Identity` is admitted with `IMAGE_PROVENANCE_BLIND_SPOT` rather than refused. **This is not a defence against a hostile registry** — it refuses a locally applied name, not content served under a name you trust. The refusal also covers *blank* and only blank — a toolchain entry reading the literal `unknown`, which the container writes itself when `WASI_VERSION_FULL` is unset, is still hashed into the key. **RE-DERIVED 2026-08-18, and the one open clause is sharpened from *"the mismatch report does not exist"* to the fact that makes it not exist.** `describeKey`'s docblock is one sentence and the row quotes it exactly — *"Human-readable, for a build log and for a mismatch report."* (`packages/aot/src/cache-key.ts:177`) — and its only non-test caller is `describeLift` in `tools/aot/lift.ts`. That much is unchanged. **What the row did not say is that there is no caller-shaped hole for a mismatch report to fill: nothing in this repository ever holds two `TranslationKey`s at once, and nothing persists one.** The type's entire production surface is six sites — the interface (`cache-key.ts:46`), `translationCid` (`:126`), `TranslationRecord` (`:171`), `translationKeyOf` (declared in `lift.ts`), the single `translationCid(translationKeyOf(lifted))` call in that same file and the single record construction beside it — and the record's only reads are inside `describeLift`. It is **never serialised**: `tools/aot/cli.ts`'s `writeFile(args.out, outcome.artifact.bytes)` writes the artifact bytes and nothing else, and `--publish-as` emits a `NameRecord` carrying `{name, cid, version, expiresAt}` — the **artifact** CID, not the key. So a `TranslationKey` lives for one `liftElf` call and is discarded. At runtime nothing holds an expected key either: `WasiExecutor` says so in its own source (`wasi-executor.ts:764-765`, *"this class holds a blockstore and a node id, never a key or a certificate"*) and `abi-router.ts` has no reference to cache keys at all. The nearest analogue is a **different subject**: `guardModuleProvenance` carries `{kind: 'cid-mismatch', name, signed, dispatched}` and names both CIDs (`module-provenance.ts:83-88`, `:105-106`) — but a CID has no fields, so there is no field-level diff there and none exists. And the hardcoded conformance CID is **test-only** (`cache-key.test.ts:53`, asserted at `:57`): its failure is vitest's diff of two base32 strings and **names no field**, which is exactly the hole that spec's own docblock describes at `:34-52` — *"each would compute a perfectly good-looking CID, they would simply never match, and nothing anywhere would report a disagreement."* **So the work splits, and only half of it is a build.** `describeMismatch(expected, actual)` is genuinely small — ~35-55 lines and a spec: `inputDigest` and `target` are string compares, `toolchain` needs a key-union walk, and `features` needs `normaliseFeatures` on both sides before a set diff, because unordered-and-duplicated is precisely what `translationCid` normalises away and what a naive compare would report as a false difference. **The caller is an owner decision**: an *expected* key has no source in the tree, so somebody must choose one — a serialised sidecar beside `--out`, a `--expect-key` argv on `tools/aot/cli.ts`, or a ruling that the conformance pin's test-only comparison is the caller and this row closes on it. Building the function alone would leave `describeKey` reachable only through `describeLift` and would not move this row **BUILT AND CLOSED 2026-08-18 BY OWNER DECISION — the expected key comes from the signed artifact record, and this row ticks.** The owner chose that source over a caller-supplied pin and over dropping the report, and the paragraph above is kept whole because every measurement in it was true when written and is the reason the decision had to be an owner's: an *expected* key had no source in the tree, so somebody had to choose one. **What was built, against the shape the paragraph above named.** `NameRecord` (`packages/core/src/naming.ts`) gains an optional `translationKeyCid`, and it is **inside the signature** — `payloadOf` folds it into the signed DAG-CBOR payload, so a translation key cannot be attached to, stripped from, or swapped on a record after the fact. `tools/aot/cli.ts`'s `--publish-as` writes it: `publishArtifact` takes the key CID and `main` hands it `outcome.artifact.translation.keyCid`, which is the serialisation the paragraph above says did not exist. `--against-record <path>` is the reader, and `tools/aot/lift.ts`'s `vouchedTranslation` is the thing that holds two at once. **Two refusal kinds, not one, and the split is the point**: `translation-key-mismatch` names the record's key CID, this lift's key CID, and renders this lift's key through `describeKey`; `record-vouches-for-no-translation` says there is nothing to compare against, which is what every record signed before today says and is an operator error with a different remedy. `guardModuleProvenance`'s `cid-mismatch` is the shape imitated deliberately — that arm says *the record is for a different artifact*, this one says *the record is for a different translation* — and the 2026-08-18 re-tag work (`image-name-not-pulled`, `IMAGE_PROVENANCE_BLIND_SPOT`) is the driver-refusal precedent followed for the new arms, including the exhaustive mapped type over `LiftFailure['kind']` in `lift.node.test.ts` that makes adding a kind without a sentence a compile error. **The limit is stated rather than left to be discovered, and it is in the refusal's own sentence**: the record carries the publisher's key **by CID and not by field**, because `TranslationKey` lives in `@o2/aot`, which depends on `@o2/core`, so carrying the four fields in a core record would either invert that dependency or duplicate an identity type. Comparing the CIDs *is* comparing the keys — `translationCid` is a pure function of the key — but only the side that was computed here can be *rendered*. The field-level diff the paragraph above scoped at 35–55 lines is therefore **not** what was built and is not claimed: what a reader gets is both keys named, one of them shown. **WHICH CLAUSE EACH PIECE OF EVIDENCE COVERS, because this requirement has two and only one was closed before today.** *Clause 1 — the cache key covering input digest, toolchain versions, target and WASM feature set, pinned to a hardcoded conformance CID*: `packages/aot/src/cache-key.test.ts` flips each of the four in turn and requires the CID to move, adds and removes a tool, reorders and duplicates the feature list, and pins `CONFORMANCE_KEY` to the literal `bafyreianou4wfzeubaqi7inz6fwuhxfrp7x3qhefrjznzasqiqs6nm34pe` — the one absolute in that file, and the only assertion there that is not relative. That clause was already met and is re-run green. *Clause 2 — the image keyed by digest so a re-tagged local image is refused*: closed 2026-08-18, proved through the spawned CLI against a real `docker tag` of the real image, with the classic-dockerd blind spot carried as `IMAGE_PROVENANCE_BLIND_SPOT` rather than as a false refusal. *The mismatch report* belongs to **clause 1** — it is what a cache key is compared *with*, and this row had held itself open on it since 2026-08-04 on the strength of `describeKey`'s own docblock. **Measured, at both levels.** `tools/aot/lift.node.test.ts` reads the predicate's three answers and asserts the two sentences do not read alike. `tools/aot/cli.node.test.ts` drives the whole thing through spawned CLI runs with no Docker: publish, then re-lift the same bytes under the same toolchain and read *"translation key agrees with the record"*; then re-lift under `clang 17.0.1` against a record signed under `16.0.6` and get exit 1 — **with the artifact bytes byte-identical to what the record vouches for**, which is the load-bearing arm, because a `cid-mismatch` cannot fire there and a byte comparison would report agreement. `packages/core/src/naming.test.ts` holds the signature: swapping, stripping and attaching the field are each refused, and a record carrying none produces a byte-identical signature to one signed before the field existed — so the demo's committed kernel records are unaffected, which is checked rather than asserted. **Two plants, watched red, restored by surgical inverse, `cmp` exit 0 against a snapshot taken immediately before each.** (1) Removing `translationKeyCid` from `payloadOf` — leaving the field on the type, in the file and in the wire form, so only the *signature* stops covering it — reddened `naming.test.ts` at *"expected true to be false"* on the swapped-key acceptance, which is the exact defect that would let a relayer edit a publisher's toolchain claim. (2) Removing the one argument that hands the key to `publishArtifact` reddened `cli.node.test.ts` twice: *"expected 'undefined' to be 'string'"* on the field's presence in the written record, and the mismatch case reporting *"the signed record for "demo/lifted" carries no translation key"* — the other arm firing, which is also the proof that the two arms are distinguishable in practice and not only in the type. **What is unchanged and is not claimed.** The classic dockerd image store still reports no `Identity` and is still admitted with a blind spot attached rather than refused; that is clause 2's stated trade and this work does not touch it. The review found a whole-job completeness predicate redundant *in this design*, on three grounds read off the tree rather than argued: the resume path already skips a carried shard without one, so a fully-carried job makes zero `planWithOffers` calls and dispatches nothing (`job/submit.ts:2861`); a safe early-out could only pay before the inputs are canonicalised, and cannot run there, because `jobId` is derived **from** those input CIDs (`jobIdOf`, `submit.ts:1288`) and `resumeState` refuses a handle whose checkpoint names another job — short-circuiting ahead of `jobId` would accept a complete checkpoint belonging to a *different* job; and the one reporting site is covered, since `bin/agent.ts:1555` prints `remaining: [...remainingWork(...)]` and an empty array **is** completeness. **The reading moved with it, measured rather than subtracted**: `unreachableExports(barrelExports(), buildCallGraph(), ROOT)` over this tree returns **66 of 250 callable barrel exports unreachable, 66 disposed, 0 open**, against the 67/66/1 above — `core/isComplete` absent from the list and `OPEN_FINDINGS` empty. The callable population fell 251 → 250 in the same change, which is the retirement itself and is stated so the two numbers are not read as independent. **A closure obtained by retiring a symbol is the one shape that could leave this guard decorative, so it was proved against rather than asserted**: two fresh plants were watched red on the committed tree — a new exported-but-uncalled function on a package barrel, which the guard named, and a stale register row for a symbol that has a caller — each restored by the surgical inverse of its own edit and `cmp`-verified at exit 0. **What this row offers the ledger guard is unchanged and still true**: `isComplete` has no production caller. Retiring an export adds no call site. What changed is that the gap is no longer *open*, because the capability was reviewed and found redundant rather than left unbuilt — and the declaration now carries that review at `packages/core/src/checkpoint.ts` so a future reader does not helpfully re-export it. `checkpoint.test.ts` is untouched and green: it imports from `./checkpoint.ts` module-relatively, so no case was lost with the advertisement. See `22-VERIFICATION.md`'s AMENDMENT of 2026-08-18 (second that day). **AMENDED 2026-08-21 (fourth amendment) — the residue is 1 again, and this cell says so rather than leaving the 2026-08-18 close standing as the final state.** `core/signNameDelegation` entered `OPEN_FINDINGS` that day as the mint half of the offline-root chain. The walked graph reaches no caller for it, and the register says why in its own words: `packages/demo/scripts/sign-kernel.ts` calls it in source, and build scripts sit outside the corpus this guard walks and are deliberately not entry points. That is a boundary of the instrument, not a claim that nothing calls the symbol, and the distinction is the whole content of the row. So the undisposed residue is **1**, `DISPOSITION_CEILING` now reads **68** against the 66 above, and the total-unreachable bound moved 66 → 67 → 68 → 69 across 2026-08-20 and 2026-08-21 with each raise named to one symbol in the comment above it. Phase 22's criterion 1 does not close on this reading. The figures above are kept because each was measured when it was written, and the correction is a re-measurement rather than an arithmetic adjustment of them |

**Re-measured 2026-08-11, and every figure in the triple had moved again inside twenty-four hours** — which is the third consecutive pass at which that has been true, and is the argument for measuring rather than citing. `73 / 225 / 36 / 37` became **`72 / 228 / 36 / 36`**. What moved, named rather than netted: **callable barrel exports 225 → 228** and **unreachable 73 → 72**, because Plan 25-02's two X.509 symbols (`core/decodeX509Certificate`, `core/describeX509Failure`) acquired a production caller when the owner ruled fail-closed and `checkX509Form` in `packages/core/src/enrollment.ts` began decoding the presented form on the trust path — genuine wiring, and the first lowering of this bound that is; against it, `net/reduceSovereignJob` **arrived** undisposed as MR-02's sovereign aggregation arm, which is why the net is −1 rather than −2. **Open 37 → 36 is the same two symbols and the same event**, and the disposed count did not move at all, correctly: wiring changes edges and dispositions change labels, and this pass changed edges. `OPEN_FINDING_CEILING` and `DISPOSITION_CEILING` both read 36 in the source and both were re-derived here rather than trusted. **The verdict is unchanged**: the guard is delivered, the residue is 36 and is held rather than worked down, and a milestone that ends with 36 unwired capabilities has not satisfied this requirement whatever the direction of the last delta.

**Re-measured 2026-08-13, and this time the INSTRUMENT moved rather than the tree — `72 / 36 disposed / 36 open` became `71 / 42 / 29`, across seven edge classes rather than five.** Every figure re-derived by this row's own method, ceilings set to 0 and the guard's own verdict read, restored by surgical inverse with `cmp` at exit 0; `36 − 7 = 29` was available and was refused as the proof. The register now reads **27** `global-object-hop`, 4 `benchmark-driver-only`, 6 `deferred-in-source`, **4** `port-member-dispatch` and **1** `proxy-trap-dispatch`, and 27+4+6+4+1 = 42 was checked rather than assumed. **The sentence this row has carried since 2026-08-11 — *"36 left OPEN with no production caller anywhere"* — was FALSE for six of them, and it is this repair that proved it rather than corrected it into agreement.** Named, because a count that moves without naming its members is how this row went stale three times: `core/executeVerified` left the population entirely and is the whole of `72 → 71`; `core/signResult`, `core/describeModuleRefusal`, `aot/describeWasiFailure` and `core/TransportError` are reached through a port interface, so the member edge lands on `packages/core/src/ports.ts#execute` and never on the implementation; `node/classifyReservationFailure` through a proxy trap; and `core/describeStartReport` is **called twice inside `packages/browser/demo/main.ts`, an entry point**, through `const { … } = await import('@o2/core')`, which resolved to a local binding and created no edge at all. **`core/executeVerified` is the one worth stopping on**: it is the fabric's N-version comparison, it runs on **every** shard dispatch from `submit.ts`'s `executeVerified(` call, and it read as dead code because its caller is a `const`-arrow and `classify` only ever called a `Function | Class | Method` callable. **This is an instrument repair and NOT wiring**, so the owner's 2026-08-08 ruling to hold the residue is untouched and no capability became reachable that was not already running — what changed is that the count stopped over-stating the gap by six, in the same direction the 2026-08-08 re-audit named about itself and the same direction `G14` named in 2026-08-10. **Two of the three blind spots were repaired as graph edges and the third was REFUSED as one**, on a measurement rather than an argument: the candidate edge for port members would have reached `demo/estimatePi` — the over-connection anchor that must stay red — because `demo/main.ts` builds its `api` object at module scope, so a disposition with a per-symbol machine-checked mechanism was taken instead. **One further claim in this file was found false in passing and is corrected rather than acted on**: `benchmark-driver-only`'s four `@o2/aot` entries say they are called only from `tools/aot/bench-lifted.ts`, and rooting `packages/aot/src/wasi-executor.ts#execute` alone flips all four — the WASI executor is built in production in `fabric-node.ts` and `browser-node.ts`. Re-causing four symbols re-opens an owner's register entry, so the measurement sits beside the sentence and the entries stay put |

| X509-01 | Phase 25 — X.509 Certificate Profile | **Done** — `id-Ed25519` (`1.3.101.112`) is the only OID `checkAlgorithm` accepts, checked at all three `AlgorithmIdentifier` positions (`TBSCertificate.signature`, `SubjectPublicKeyInfo.algorithm`, `Certificate.signatureAlgorithm`); every other OID refuses `unrecognised-algorithm` by construction, not by a deny-list. Proved by `x509.test.ts`'s golden-fixture acceptance test (Ed25519 decodes correctly) and the four named-ban tests below. **Wired 2026-08-11, fail-closed, on the owner ruling recorded above this table's family heading.** `verifyCertificate` (`packages/core/src/enrollment.ts`) is the production caller: it decodes the optional `x509` field of every certificate handed to it, before any signature work, and forwards this profile's typed refusal unchanged as `x509-profile-refused`. Proved *on the trust path* rather than against the decoder — `x509.test.ts`'s `describe('X509-01 — a non-Ed25519 algorithm is refused on the trust path')` runs a certificate whose `SubjectPublicKeyInfo` algorithm is `rsaEncryption`, and one carrying a `NULL` `parameters` field on the Ed25519 `AlgorithmIdentifier`, through `verifyCertificate` itself. **Every fixture is re-signed by the pinned provider**, so its envelope signature verifies perfectly and the gate is the only thing that refuses it; a fixture signed any other way would have been shadowed by the signature check that already existed and would have proved nothing. Issuance is the other half and is opt-in: `AuthorityOptions.x509` mints the form, guarded by `enrollment.test.ts`'s `describe('X509-01 — a provider can issue the profile\\'s X.509 form alongside the envelope')`, whose first case is the non-vacuity control — a conformant form the production encoder minted is **accepted** |
| X509-02 | Phase 25 — X.509 Certificate Profile | **Done** — SHA-1 (`sha1WithRSAEncryption`), P-192 and P-224 (both via `id-ecPublicKey` with the curve OID in `parameters` — the outer OID alone refuses, so two distinct planted fixtures reach the identical `unrecognised-algorithm` path, satisfying "each with its own test") and RSA (`rsaEncryption`) are each refused by name, in `x509.test.ts`'s `describe('algorithm allow-list (X509-01/02)', ...)` block, four separate tests, each asserting the specific OID in `result.failure.oid`. **Wired 2026-08-11, fail-closed** — and the four bans are re-proved through `verifyCertificate` rather than inherited from the decoder's own suite, in `x509.test.ts`'s `describe('X509-02 — SHA-1, P-192, P-224 and RSA are each refused by name on the trust path')`: four separate cases, each planting its OID into a certificate the **pinned** provider then signs, each asserting the forwarded `unrecognised-algorithm` carries that exact OID. The P-192 and P-224 cases still refuse by the outer `id-ecPublicKey` OID, which is the same mechanism this row already recorded — what changed is where the refusal lands, which is now a certificate being denied rather than a decoder returning a value |
| X509-03 | Phase 25 — X.509 Certificate Profile | **Done** — `checkDerCanonical` (Plan 25-01) is wired as `decodeX509Certificate`'s final gate, after every structural/algorithm/extension check, so a semantic refusal still fires specifically rather than every malformed case collapsing into one generic reason. Proved at whole-certificate scope (not just the standalone-TLV scope Plan 25-01 proved) by three planted mutations in `x509.test.ts`'s `describe('certificate-level DER canonicalisation gate (X509-03)', ...)`: a non-minimal `signatureValue` length, a redundant leading `0x00` on `serialNumber`, and unzeroed `BIT STRING` padding bits on `subjectPublicKey`, each refusing `non-canonical-encoding`. **Wired 2026-08-11, fail-closed**, and re-proved through `verifyCertificate` in `x509.test.ts`'s `describe('X509-03 — a non-canonical certificate is refused on the trust path')`: a certificate whose `signatureValue` length uses long form where short form would fit, serialized through the test's `encodeDerRaw` because the profile's own `encodeDer` would repair the mutation on the way out — the gate is shown refusing bytes that really are non-canonical on the wire, not bytes a test only intended to be. **The canonicality gate additionally became load-bearing for a second reason** when the profile was wired: `x509-encode.ts` emits through the same `encodeDer`, so an issued form is canonical by construction, and `verifyCertificate` compares the presented `TBSCertificate` byte-for-byte against a re-encoding of the envelope — a comparison that would be meaningless if two encodings of one certificate could both be legal |
| X509-04 | Phase 25 — X.509 Certificate Profile | **Done** — `MAX_CERTIFICATE_BYTES` (Plan 25-01) is checked by `preParseCheck` before `decodeDer` runs at all, proved by `x509.test.ts`'s `describe('MAX_CERTIFICATE_BYTES is a bound, not a ban (X509-04)', ...)` (at-the-bound accepted, one-past refused by name) and the ordering proof in `describe('certificate-size gate runs before any TLV parsing (X509-04)', ...)` (a `decodeDer` call-count assertion of zero). **Wired 2026-08-11, fail-closed**, and re-proved through `verifyCertificate` in `x509.test.ts`'s `describe('X509-04 — a certificate past the byte ceiling is refused on the trust path, before any parsing')`, which pads a conformant form past the ceiling and asserts the forwarded `certificate-too-large` carries the real size and the limit. **This ceiling is the one obligation the gate's other checks could not stand in for**, and that is why it runs first: the whole-`TBSCertificate` byte comparison and the X.509 signature check would both refuse an oversized blob too, but only after it had been parsed, which is the work the bound exists to refuse. Also proved at the **wire** in `net/src/enrol-protocol.test.ts`'s `describe('X509-04 — the X.509 form survives the wire, and a malformed one is not laundered')`: the form crosses `encodeResponse`/`parseResponse` intact, a non-string `x509` refuses the whole frame rather than being dropped, and an altered one reaches the gate and is refused by the profile's own name |
| X509-05 | Phase 25 — X.509 Certificate Profile | **Done** — `export const MAX_CHAIN_DEPTH = 8` in `capability.ts`, enforced by `verifyChain`'s `if (chain.length > MAX_CHAIN_DEPTH)` before any signature work, its companion `Math.min(...spread)` RangeError hazard closed by the `reduce` the same file's docblock describes — *"The fold below is now a `reduce`, so **neither control depends on the other**"*. `verifyChain` is reachable through `authorizeCapability` (`packages/net/src/capability-authorizer.ts`), installed by both `FabricNode` and `browser-node.ts` — a real production call path, unlike the other six rows here. Proved by `capability.test.ts`'s three-part bound (accepts at the bound, refuses one past it by name, refuses a 250,000-link forged chain instead of throwing), whose `describe` block Plan 25-02 relabelled `'chain depth is bounded (X509-05 — max chain depth, delivered here, not re-implemented)'` so this row's evidence is a title the ledger's own traceability audit can read, rather than an unlabelled block a reader has to take on faith |
| X509-06 | Phase 25 — X.509 Certificate Profile | **Done** — `too-many-extensions` (`MAX_EXTENSION_COUNT`) is checked against the parsed extension-list length before any `extnValue` is interpreted; `extension-too-large` (`MAX_EXTENSION_BYTES`) is checked per extension before its dag-cbor payload is decoded. Proved by `x509.test.ts`'s two-sided bounds in `describe('extension rules (X509-06/07) and custom extension mapping', ...)` — since this profile's closed 4-member registry cannot build `MAX_EXTENSION_COUNT` genuinely-legal extensions, each "accepted at the bound" case is proved at the mechanism level (the count/size gate passing, evidenced by the next refusal being `unrecognised-extension` rather than the bound's own kind), and each "one past the bound" case refuses by name with the count/bytes and the limit. **Wired 2026-08-11, fail-closed**, and both ceilings re-proved through `verifyCertificate` in `x509.test.ts`'s `describe('X509-06 — extension count and size ceilings are refused on the trust path')`: `MAX_EXTENSION_COUNT + 1` extensions grafted into a certificate the pinned provider then signs, refused `too-many-extensions` with the count and the limit; and one extension one byte past `MAX_EXTENSION_BYTES`, refused `extension-too-large` naming its OID — the size kind rather than `unrecognised-extension`, which is what shows the size gate really runs before the payload is looked at |
| X509-07 | Phase 25 — X.509 Certificate Profile | **Done** — a duplicate `extnID` is checked against a `Set` on the first repeat seen during the single pass over the extension list, before that extension's size or payload is read — no last-wins, no warning. Proved by `x509.test.ts`'s `'refuses a duplicate extnID outright, never last-wins'` case (two `EXT_USER_KEY` extensions, refuses `duplicate-extension` naming the OID). **Wired 2026-08-11, fail-closed**, and re-proved through `verifyCertificate` in `x509.test.ts`'s `describe('X509-07 — a duplicate extension is refused outright on the trust path')`, which appends a second copy of the `userKey` extension to a certificate the pinned provider then signs and asserts the forwarded `duplicate-extension` names `EXT_USER_KEY`. **Never last-wins matters more once wired than it did before**: last-wins here would mean two statements about which user a node belongs to, with the relying party silently taking the second |

The gate returned **NO-GO** (`26-GATE.md`): 21 of 27 non-test translation units do not compile for `wasm32-wasi`, and the cause is **glog**, which branches on Windows, Cygwin, Linux, Android, macOS, the BSDs and Emscripten and has **no `__wasi__` branch at all** — the `wasm32-wasi-threads` arm moves the refusal from `glog/logging.h:51` to `glog/platform.h:58` and removes nothing. No `elflift.wasm` was linked, so no bitcode was produced and no `sha256` comparison against native elfconv was possible. The symbol half of the gate is separately an **upper bound and conclusive in neither direction**, since `wasm-ld` pulls only the members it needs and the whole process and signal residue lives in `Program.cpp.o`, `Signals.cpp.o`, `CrashRecoveryContext.cpp.o`, `Process.cpp.o`, `LockFileManager.cpp.o` and `Watchdog.cpp.o` of LLVMSupport. **What blocks this id is a cross-build nobody has paid**: precondition 3 of `26-CONTEXT.md` records that the only cross-compiled LLVM on this host is Emscripten-targeted and cannot be guest code under this project's preview1 sandbox, so a wasm32-wasi LLVM must be built from source before the deliverable is reachable at all. **CORRECTED 2026-08-14, and the correction is against this project's own interest.** A session note of that date recorded the opposite of the sentence above — that glog was never the wall, that a 2-line `__wasi__` branch plus about seven lines fixing elfconv's own bugs (`Lift.cpp:61`'s `siginfo_t` outside its guard; `Loader.h`'s `uintptr_t` guest addresses) gets **27/27** translation units compiling at 14.3 MB of objects — and that note was about to be transcribed into this row as fact. It was checked first, and it does not survive: **the measurement was never preserved in any executable form.** A repo-wide search for `27/27|27 of 27|14.3 MB` returns exactly two prose sentences, in `.planning/.continue-here.md` and `.planning/STATE.md`, both landed by the SAME commit `eb084c5` (*chore: pause handoff*). There is no patch file, no `gate.json`, no build log, no diff and no fixture; `tools/aot/` has no wasi-patch script; and `git -C third_party/elfconv status` reports the submodule **pristine** at its pinned commit, so the two-line branch and the two bug fixes are not in this repository. The 27/27 result is therefore **not reproducible from this tree** and is recorded as a session observation in a working tree that was not kept — which is a different thing from a measurement, and the difference is exactly what this ledger exists to hold. The NO-GO verdict and its `glog` attribution stand as the last *reproducible* reading. What does not change either way is the blocker named above: the wasm32-wasi LLVM cross-build is unpaid, and nothing in the session note claims otherwise |
| EGR-01 | Phase 27 — The Demo UI, Driven by the Real Fabric | **Done** — the egress reading has three arms and each names which fact it is stating. **The fix is one field and one branch, and the field is where it is because the deferred note pointed at the wrong place.** That note said to count where the guard registers a sovereign CID; `EgressGuard.guard()` appends no `EgressEntry`, `submitJobWithEgress` gives every hold back in a `finally`, and `sliceManifest` recomputes from `entries` alone, so a figure read off the guard after a job is zero. **The count is taken in `submitJobWithEgress`** — the only expression that knows a job's sovereign shard set — beside the registration itself, and travels through `sliceManifest` as a **required** third argument. It follows `totalBytes`' mechanism (a figure taken over the manifest's own window, the guard's over the whole record and the slice's over one job) and deliberately not its implementation, because a registration leaves no entry to reduce over; the parameter is required rather than defaulted because a caller that omitted it would silently produce the manifest that says a sovereign run registered nothing. **The three arms, as rendered on screen** (`byo/egress`, read off the harness that first printed the defect): nothing registered — `0 withheld — and this run registered no sovereign data, so that is the guard reporting it had nothing to hold back, not a proof of sovereignty.`; registered and clean — `0 withheld — and this run registered 6 sovereign shards, so the guard was watching for those bytes and saw none of them leave. That is a clean scan of what crossed, not a proof about what could not.`; withheld — unchanged, `N frame(s) WITHHELD: <ids>. Those bytes are still on this device. They were not sent anywhere.` The middle arm is worded as a **detector's** report and not a proof, matching what the scan in `egress.ts` actually is. **The contradiction G13 named is gone in the same render pass it was found in**: `byo/sovereign-label = sovereign — every shard was submitted owner-pinned to public.` now sits beside the middle arm rather than beside the first. **A passing spec was holding the defect, and replacing it was half the work.** `demo-byo.e2e.test.ts` required the false words whenever `violations` was empty; it is replaced by an assertion of both facts separately — `registeredSovereign` must equal the shards the arm submitted, and the *registered no sovereign data* sentence is **forbidden** on a run that registered some — and the same pair was added to the unplaceable arm, where the old sentence was most obviously false because nothing was placed and nothing left. **Watched red rather than reasoned about, three plants.** Planting the two-arm branch back (`registeredSovereign === 0` → `>= 0`) reddened both byo sovereign cases with *"the page told a visitor this run registered no sovereign data, on a dispatch that registered 6 — the two facts are conflated again"* and *"Y13 says every shard was submitted owner-pinned and the egress region says the run registered no sovereign data — one render pass, two contradictory readings"*; the same plant reddened the formatter case in `demo-fabric.e2e.test.ts`. Planting the count away (`registeredSovereign += 1` → `+= 0`) reddened `submit-with-egress.test.ts` with `expected [ +0, +0 ] to deeply equal [ 3, 3 ]`. **The first plant is also this row's own correction against itself**: it initially reddened on a *mechanism* assertion ordered before the claim, leaving the claim unproved by the run that was meant to prove it — the CRYPTO-01 failure repeated — so the negative was moved above the positives and the plant re-applied, which is the reading recorded here. All restored by surgical inverse, `cmp` exit 0 against pre-plant snapshots. **Guarded going forward on two tiers**: `submit-with-egress.test.ts` proves the count is the run's and not the guard's — three shards over two distinct rows on two guards reads `[3, 3]` where counting labels gives 2, counting holds across guards gives 6 and reading `guard.registrations` after the job gives 0 — and `demo-fabric.e2e.test.ts` proves the two `0 withheld` arms differ by the count alone, asserting the frames and bytes lines are identical between them. **Files:** `packages/net/src/egress.ts`, `packages/net/src/submit-with-egress.ts`, `packages/browser/demo/render.ts`, `packages/node/src/demo-region-properties.ts` (`EGRESS_SENTENCES` is three entries, matched by `includes`, so each is a phrase no other arm contains), UI-SPEC §5.2 amended in place with its prior bullets preserved to the character, plus `surfaces/fabric.ts`'s inherited-limit paragraph, which stated a falsified claim and is a comment this project fixes rather than leaves. Measured: `tsc --noEmit` exit 0 · `--project node` 180 files / 2615 passed / 1 skipped, exit 0 · `--project e2e` 28 files / 185 passed, exit 0, each exit code read directly. **Three things are NOT claimed.** P7 sees rendered text and no manifest, so it checks that *a* sentence is present and never that the *right* one is — the factual half is the byo spec's, and P7's docblock says so. `EgressGuard.manifest`'s own `registeredSovereign` is a whole-record tally cleared by `reset()`, honest for its window and **not** job-scoped; nothing in production renders it, and its docblock forbids reading it as a run's. And the middle arm rests on the raw-byte scan, which `egress.ts` states is a detector and not a predictor — the sentence says *a clean scan of what crossed*, which is exactly as much as the mechanism supports |

| CRYPTO-06 | Phase 28 — One Cryptographic Implementation | **Done** — the WebKit finding is two guards rather than a docblock. **The finding, restated:** signing the same message with the same key on the same page yields different Ed25519 signature bytes per engine, because nonce construction is engine-specific. `describe('cross-arm signing is mutually verifiable, never byte-identical (CRYPTO-06)')` runs four seeds — 7, 11, 13, 17 — and asserts all four verification directions on each: noble to noble, noble to subtle, subtle to subtle, subtle to noble. **Every direction passed on every engine.** The per-engine byte-match verdict, as printed: node v25.9.0 **MATCHED x4**, chromium **MATCHED x4**, firefox **MATCHED x4**, webkit **DIFFERED x4**. Four seeds rather than one, so webkit's divergence reads as its nonce construction and not as a coincidence on a single input. **Byte-equality of Ed25519 signatures is asserted in neither direction, deliberately**: `toEqual` would be red four times on webkit and `not.toEqual` twelve times across the other three, so either would encode an engine rather than a property, and the acceptance grep confirms neither is present. **X25519 is the contrast case and the only byte-identity claim in the block** — `agreeX25519` over the same key pair *is* asserted byte-identical across arms and passes on all three engines, without which the block would read as "cross-arm agreement is generally impossible", which is false. **The source-level half** is `one-crypto-implementation.node.test.ts` Block 2: `findSignatureIdentityConstructs` raises equality, keyed-collection and conversion-as-key constructs over the same 153-file corpus, with two exclusions written for lines this tree really contains. Nine candidates were raised, eight excluded, **one kept** — `tools/aot/cli.ts`, `readBack.signature !== record.signature`, a serialisation-fidelity check inside one process with one signing operation, which the hedged-nonce finding cannot reach because there is no second engine for it to differ from. The register is anchored by file plus source text rather than by line number, and `SIGNATURE_COMPARISON_CEILING` is **2**, asserted to be exactly one more than the register length. **Set equality is asserted in both directions and each direction was watched red**: disabling an exclusion produced three unregistered findings and 4 failed of 24, and changing the anchor text to an expression the tree does not contain produced one stale entry and 3 failed of 24 — so an unregistered finding and a stale permission both fail |

| NET-13 | v2.0 — Phase 34 | **Done** — 2026-09-02. **The specification states it, in one authoritative place.** `RELAY_DATA_LIMIT_BYTES` (`packages/libp2p/src/constants.ts:101`) read *"in total, in each direction"* — two readings at once, and the generous one is the one a design takes. Its docblock now carries the correction and the primary source: `@libp2p/circuit-relay-v2/dist/src/utils.js`'s `createLimitedRelay` builds **one** `dataLimit` object and hands the same object to `countStreamBytes` for each direction, so bytes out and bytes back decrement one number. `relayedBudgetPerDirection()` (`:115`) is the per-direction figure **derived** from the limit rather than transcribed beside it, so the fabric has one number and it cannot drift. Both docblocks say the figures are defaults **of the relay server**, set by whoever runs it. **The boundary test drives the relayed path** — `packages/node/src/relayed-budget.node.test.ts`, a real `circuitRelayServer()` started by `FabricNode` on the shipped defaults with **no `dataLimitBytes` override anywhere in the file**, so the constant sits on the used side of `fabric-node.ts:2211`'s `??` and a mutation of the shipped figure is reachable. Two peers listening on nothing but `/p2p-circuit`, exchanging over the fabric's own protocol. Measured: 62 KiB each way completes byte-identical (`:265`); **64 KiB each way — the size a "128 KiB per direction" reading calls safe — is cut** (`:295`), request whole, echo back 49 152 bytes, circuit gone. That echo figure is the same one the hosted relay returned for the same 16 KiB framing on 2026-08-24 (consult §15), so two unrelated relays agree to the byte. A 1 KiB sweep puts this connection's non-payload cost between 2 049 and 4 097 bytes — the counter is on **wire** bytes — which makes `relayedBudgetPerDirection()` a ceiling that is **not reachable**, and the constant says so rather than inviting a protocol to size at it. **The row's own trap is closed by a positive control, not by an assertion**: `:343` runs the identical exchange at the identical size over a **direct** connection and it completes, which is what makes the green above a statement about the relay rather than about the size. **And the observation cannot be faked by a truncated delivery.** This transport's message boundary is *the stream ended* — no length prefix — so the relay's cut arrives as a short message the receiving transport dispatches as complete with `refusedInbound` 0 (49 152 bytes at 64 KiB, **0 bytes** at 63 KiB). *Did a reply arrive* is therefore a question the cut answers yes to, and every assertion is on the bytes and on whether the circuit survived. **Plants.** `fabric-node.ts:2211`'s `?? RELAY_DATA_LIMIT_BYTES` → `?? 1_073_741_824n`: the boundary case alone went red — *"Error: timed out waiting for the relay to cut the circuit"* — with the in-budget case and the direct control still green. `isControlPath` → `return false`: four cases red, three of them *"expected 'carries-work' to be 'control-only'"*. The path-class gate → `'carries-work'`: *"expected 'send-failed' to be 'send-refused'"*. All three restored by the surgical inverse and verified `cmp`-clean against a snapshot taken immediately before each plant. **One assertion is recorded as NOT carrying its claim**: the refusal case's elapsed-time bound stayed green through its plant, because with the gate open the relay cut the circuit and the send failed in 173 ms rather than stalling — so what the gate buys is *attribution*, not speed, and the case rests on the kind and the reason instead. **`relayedBudgetPerDirection` is deliberately not on the `@o2/libp2p` barrel** (`packages/libp2p/src/index.ts`): its only consumer is `Libp2pTransport.send` inside that package, and publishing a surface nothing outside consumes is the cost the crypto-backend factories are kept off the barrel to avoid. Measured rather than assumed — `Libp2pTransport.send` is in the call graph and **unreachable** from all five entry points, because it is only ever reached through the `Transport` interface, which is the reachability guard's own documented blind spot; `UNREACHABLE_CEILING` did not move |
| NET-14 | v2.0 — Phase 32 | **Done against the amended criterion, with its granularity stated** — `TrafficSplitCounter` (`packages/libp2p/src/traffic-split.ts`) keeps connection-seconds and bytes in two columns, fed from `trackMultiaddrConnection`, which `libp2p/dist/src/upgrader.js:140` calls on every upgrade in both directions — the one seam every transport passes through. Wired into the hosted assembly and reported as a **field on `/self`**, not a second route, and held by the Durable Object rather than by the lazily-built fabric so the split reads **before the relay carries anything**: measured on a real workerd as two zeroed columns before any peer dialled and a moved `direct` column with `relayed` still exactly zero after eight did. Criterion 4's non-vacuity is `traffic-split.e2e.test.ts`, two arrangements against a real circuit relay. Planted by making `trackMultiaddrConnection` a no-op — both arrangements red at *no bytes reached the direct column within 20 s* — and restored `cmp`-clean. **One plant STAYED GREEN and is recorded rather than dropped**: swapping the classifier's WebRTC-first order changed nothing, because `@multiformats/multiaddr-matcher@3.0.2` already answers `Circuit.matches` **false** for `<relay>/p2p-circuit/webrtc/p2p/<self>` — measured, against the expectation that it would not. The ordering is kept for intent and the property is held instead by a case that pins the *library's* answer, so a version that starts matching that form as a circuit reddens there rather than silently reporting every direct browser pair as relayed. **THE GRANULARITY, and it is three separate limits rather than one hedge.** (1) *What the reporting surface answers.* `/self`'s split is about the connections **this node holds** — how much of ITS OWN traffic is relayed — and not about traffic it **relays for others**. Those are different questions. The hosted node dials through no relay, so its `relayed` column is structurally zero and the e2e pins it at zero after eight dials; relayed payload transits this node as circuit **hop/stop protocol streams riding direct WebSocket legs**, and `trackProtocolStream` is deliberately inert. So when Phase 32 criteria 1–2 land and two browsers meet through the deployed relay, every byte it carries is counted **direct** by this surface. (2) *Where the other half lands.* Relay-carried bytes are hop-stream counting, and they belong with Phase 32 criteria 1 and 2, which is the first arrangement in which they are observable at all. Building them now would be building against no reading. (3) *The counters are per-instance and reset on eviction.* A Durable Object is reconstructed constantly, and a hibernation-woken socket is closed with `CLOSED_AFTER_HIBERNATION` (1012) and must be redialled — so a redial re-enters the upgrader and starts a fresh count, while the pre-eviction connection's seconds and bytes go with the instance. The split is a **live** reading, not a lifetime total, and it is therefore not a billing total either. What IS delivered and read: the two columns exist and are non-vacuous across two arrangements. **DEPLOYED AND VERIFIED 2026-08-31** — `v2.0.0-rc.5` shipped through CI on the `release: published` trigger, and `https://o2-bootstrap.af-4a0.workers.dev/self` answers `version: 2.0.0-rc.5` with `traffic: {direct: {0,0}, relayed: {0,0}}` on PeerId `12D3KooWKm587fnGat5xncq9kaWUk4bN5gUJQiF4q8EwJnrb7rsz` — the same identity it has carried since 2026-08-27, so this reading is also a fourth crossing of a redeploy boundary for `HOST-01`. **AND ONE CLAIM IS WITHDRAWN IN THE SAME BREATH.** This row said the split reports *before the relay carries anything*. That is true of the reporting and NOT of the ordering Phase 32 criterion 3 asks for. The basis recorded for it — that nothing publishes the relay's address to a browser — is **false**: `bootstrap.json` publishes `relayAddrs` naming this node, and `browser-node.ts:597` listens on `['/p2p-circuit', '/webrtc']`, which is a reservation request. The published client has been able to join since `v2.0.0-rc.4` on 2026-08-28, three days before these counters existed. Whether a browser reserved in that window cannot be read from here — the counters are per-instance and hold no history. So the ordering is **unverified and may be permanently false**, the same shape as `HOST-10`, and it is written down rather than left standing on a basis that does not hold **AMENDED 2026-08-30 — two of the three limits are retired, one is not, and the withdrawn ordering claim does not move.** Limit (2) said hop-stream counting *"belongs with Phase 32 criteria 1 and 2, which is the first arrangement in which they are observable at all"*. That was wrong about the arrangement rather than about the counting: a real `circuitRelayServer` in an e2e puts hop and stop streams through `trackProtocolStream` in both directions with no deployed relay and no browsers. `trackProtocolStream` is no longer inert — `packages/libp2p/src/relay-service-log.ts` recognises the two codecs and keeps FOUR direction-separated counters, because a relay someone used and a relay this node used are the same protocol string and the direction is the whole meaning. `/self` carries it as a THIRD reading, `relayService`, never folded into the split's two columns: `direct` already contains every byte this relay forwards, `relayService.bytes` counts that same payload at a different question, and the two must not be reconciled by subtraction. Limit (3) said the counters are per-instance and *"not a lifetime total"*. Still true of `traffic`; now false of the tier — `packages/cloudflare/src/relay-service-journal.ts` banks the relay record into Durable Object storage under `/journal/relay-service`, one key holding six numbers rather than an append-only log, and REFUSES any write that would shorten it, which turns the fresh-instance-writes-zeros bug into an error rather than a discipline. Limit (1) is untouched and is still how the split must be read. **The marker is `firstInboundHopStreamAt` and not `firstReservationAt`**: a hop stream carries RESERVE or CONNECT and the wire cannot tell them apart, and the relay server emits no event — measured, `dispatchEvent` appears nowhere under its `dist/src/server/`. Verified: 14 unit cases on the log, 14 on the journal against the real `DoDatastore`, 2 e2e against a real `circuitRelayServer`, and `relay-service-journal.e2e.test.ts` — which reserves against a real workerd, KILLS AND RESTARTS wrangler, and reads the record back from a new process while `traffic` reads zero in the same answer. Seven plants watched, five red; the two that stayed green are recorded beside the cases that carry their claims instead. **The withdrawn ordering claim is NOT reinstated** — nothing here reconstructs history it did not observe, so the `rc.4`→`rc.5` window stays dark and criterion 3 remains unverified and possibly permanently false. What changed is that the next such question has an answer. |

The pitfall this row was written against is `.planning/research/v2.0/PITFALLS.md` §13 |

**AMENDED 2026-08-30 — the sentence above is kept because it is a claim with a date on it, and
the date is what has expired.** *"All 36 are unchecked and every one of their verdicts begins
`Not started`"* was a true reading of the table on 2026-08-25 and is false now: **nine** of the
36 are ticked — `HOST-01`, `HOST-05`, `HOST-08`, `HOST-11`, `HOST-12`, `HOST-15`, `NET-11`,
`DEMO-05` and `DEMO-06`. Six of those nine were ticked on 2026-08-30 as *corrections*: the work
had landed and the rows had not moved with it, which is the drift this whole paragraph exists to
warn about, arriving inside the paragraph's own subject. `HOST-10` is the one row whose verdict
is **`Refuted`** rather than `Not started` or `Done`, and its checkbox stays off for the reason
the legend gives — `startsWith('Done')` is the rule, and a refuted criterion is not a met one.
The mapping figure of 138 of 138 is unaffected: ticking a box changes no id. Read the figures by
the `grep -c` commands rather than by this paragraph, which will expire again.
